  `updateTenantRateLimit` validates with zod (same bounds as the table),
  requires AAL2, upserts under RLS and writes `RATE_LIMIT_UPDATED`. Login, MFA
  and tenant creation keep the platform's limits: they protect authentication
  itself, and before a session the tenant is not verified.

**Supabase Auth native limits:** `[auth.rate_limit]` in `supabase/config.toml`
writes down the values the hosted project must carry (email 2/h,
token_refresh 150, sign_in_sign_ups 30, token_verifications 30 per 5 min…),
so a CLI upgrade cannot change them silently. Locally GoTrue receives them
but does not enforce the per-IP ones: the CLI sets no rate-limit header, and
without one GoTrue skips them. Checked: 170 wrong passwords in a row against
local Auth got no 429. Locally, the app's limiter is the one that acts.

**Employees search filter:** `lib/postgrest/filters.ts` builds `.or()` values
inside double quotes (with `\` and `"` escaped), where `,` `.` `:` `(` `)` are
plain text, and escapes the LIKE wildcards. `*` is dropped, because PostgREST
turns it into `%`. The search text is validated with zod (trimmed, at most 100
characters, page ≥ 1). `performance/page.tsx` uses the same builder. A static
test fails on any `.or()`, `.filter()` or `.not()` built from a template
literal or string concatenation.

**Tests:**
- unit: policies, hashed keys, fail-closed vs fail-open, last-hop IP, messages,
  429 + Retry-After; filter builders and the search schema; the Admin Center
  action (bounds, AAL2, permission, upsert, audit);
- static: every exported Server Action calls a limiter, every route handler
  calls `limitApi()` (health excepted), no raw PostgREST filters;
- integration, against Supabase local:
  - `rate_limit_consume` as `prisma_app`, window reset and tenant overrides;
  - the function cannot be called from the Data API, anonymous or signed in;
  - RLS and bounds of `tenant_rate_limits`;
  - the real `signIn` and `verifyMfa` against Supabase Auth: the sixth
    attempt is refused even with the right password or code, one IP is limited
    across accounts, login fails closed when the limiter is unreachable, and
    the login is audited on the server;
  - the fourth tenant creation in an hour is refused;
  - `"a,id.eq.1)("` returns no row and no error, where the old filter ran the
    injected `id.eq.1`.

**Accepted for now:**
- The Supabase Auth API stays public (the browser client needs it), so a
  client calling it directly with the anon key skips the app's limiter. Only
  the native per-IP limits protect that path, and they act only on the hosted
  project. The Auth hooks that would close it (password and MFA verification
  attempts) need a paid plan, so they are not used (Principle 8).
- Since login and MFA run on the server, hosted Supabase Auth would count every
  attempt against the server's IP. Phase 23 must forward the client IP with
  `Sb-Forwarded-For` (secret API key, `security_sb_forwarded_for_enabled`),
  or all users share one bucket.
- Without a proxy, `X-Forwarded-For` comes from the client, so the per-IP
  bucket is a first barrier only. The account bucket is not affected.
- The account bucket lets an attacker block a victim's login while they keep
  sending wrong passwords; the victim can sign in again one window (60 s) after
  the attacker stops.
- Fixed windows allow up to twice the limit across a window boundary.
- There is no password-reset flow yet (it arrives with Resend in Phase 23). When
  it is built, its Server Action is caught by the coverage test until it spends
  a token. Supabase Auth's `/recover` keeps its native per-user and email limits.
- Each limited call adds one database round trip; `write` and `api` also read
  the session claims.

---

## D29 — Content-Security-Policy with Nonces

**Decision:** the middleware sends a Content-Security-Policy with a fresh nonce
on every response it handles. Scripts run only with that nonce, with no
`'unsafe-inline'` and no `'unsafe-eval'` outside `next dev`. The browser build
ships no source maps. Phase 9b, PR 4.

- `lib/security/csp.ts` builds the policy. Scripts get `'self' 'nonce-<n>'
  'strict-dynamic'`: a nonced script may load the chunks it imports, and
  nothing else runs. `next dev` adds `'unsafe-eval'`, which React's
  development build needs. The other directives are the ones `next.config.ts`
  used to send.
- `src/middleware.ts` makes a 128-bit nonce per request (Web Crypto, Edge
  runtime). It sets the policy on the request, where Next.js reads the nonce to
  put on its own scripts, and on the response, where the browser enforces it.
  It also forwards the nonce as `x-nonce`, replacing any value the client sent.
- `next.config.ts` no longer sends a CSP. Two policies would both apply, and
  one without the nonce would block every script. The other security headers
  stay there.
- The root layout calls `connection()`, so every page renders per request. A
  page prerendered at build time would carry no nonce and break. The build
  lists every route as dynamic.
- `productionBrowserSourceMaps: false` is explicit; the build check of D32
  fails if a `.map` file appears in `.next/static`.

**Tests:**
- unit tests for the policy (no `'unsafe-inline'`, `'unsafe-eval'` only in
  dev, fresh nonces) and for the middleware (same nonce in the header and
  forwarded to the page, a new one per request, set on redirects too);
- `scripts/check-csp.mjs`: CI asks the running web image for `/login` and fails
  unless the policy has a nonce and every `<script>` carries it;
- in the production image, a browser visited 11 pages (login, the employee
  modules, performance forms, MFA and Admin Center) with no CSP violation. An
  inline script injected into the HTML was blocked, and `eval()` threw
  `EvalError`.

**Accepted for now:**
- Styles keep `'unsafe-inline'`: React and Next.js write inline style
  attributes, and a nonce does not cover attributes.
- Files outside the middleware (static assets, `/api/health`) carry no CSP;
  none of them is HTML.
- Rendering every page per request rules out static pages; all of them already
  depend on the session.

---

## D30 — Server-Only Modules and the Client Boundary

**Decision:** every module that holds a key or a privileged connection starts
with `import "server-only"`. A client component that imports one, directly or
through other modules, fails the build, and a lint rule of our own reports it
first, with the chain of imports. Phase 9b, PR 4.

- **Marked:** `lib/db/admin-client.ts` (superuser), `lib/db/tenant-client.ts`
  (`prisma_app`), `lib/email/index.ts` (`email_enqueuer`) and
  `lib/lms/moodle-client.ts` (`MOODLE_WS_TOKEN`). Its runtime `window` check is
  replaced by the marker. Everything that uses them (`can()`, the rate limiter,
  the LMS sync…) is covered through them.
- **Lint:** `eslint-rules/no-server-only-in-client.mjs`
  (`scalia/no-server-only-in-client`):
  - it applies to files that start with `"use client"`;
  - it follows local imports (`@/…` and relative, not `import type`) until it
    finds the marker;
  - it stops at `"use server"` files, because a client only gets a reference
    to a Server Action;
  - it also refuses `server-only`, `@prisma/client`, `pg` and `pg-boss`
    imported directly.
- **Tests:** Vitest resolves `server-only` to the package's own server build
  (empty), the same thing the `react-server` condition does in Next.js. The web
  and worker configs both do this, since the worker suite imports `lib/email`.

**Tests:** the rule against a fixture tree:
- a direct import, a chain of two files, packages, dynamic imports and
  re-exports are refused;
- Server Action files, type-only imports, plain modules and server components
  pass.

---

## D31 — Zod in Every Server Action

**Decision:** every Server Action that takes input validates it with zod
before using it, and only real Server Actions carry `"use server"`. Phase 9b,
PR 4.

- `createGoal` and `sendFeedback` read FormData through
  `lib/validations/performance.ts`:
  - required title and dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), with the end date not before the
    start;
  - weight from 0 to 1, numeric target and length limits;
  - feedback with a recipient id, content and the database's enum values;
  - empty optional fields become null;
  - anything that is not FormData is rejected.
- `switchTenant` and `revokeMembership` validate the tenant id with
  `z.guid()` instead of a hand-written regex. The seed's ids (`a0000000-…`) are
  not RFC 4122 v4, which `z.uuid()` would refuse. D2's "Zod + DB" now holds.
- `lms-sync`, `provision-tenant` and `get-module-flags` drop `"use server"`.
  They are server helpers, but with the directive every export was a Server
  Action that a client could call with any arguments.
- `__tests__/static/zod-coverage.test.ts`:
  - it lists the files that may carry `"use server"`;
  - it fails when an exported Server Action with parameters calls neither
    `.safeParse(` nor `.parse(`.

---

## D32 — No Debug or Test Routes in the Production Build

**Decision:** CI inspects the production build right after `next build` and
fails on any debug or test route and on browser source maps. Phase 9b, PR 4.

- `scripts/check-prod-build.mjs` (`npm run check:prod-build`) reads the app
  router's and the pages router's route manifests. It refuses a route when a
  segment, split on `-` `_` `.`, is one of: debug, test(s), testing, dev,
  devtools, playground, sandbox, mock(s), fixture(s), e2e, seed(s) or phpinfo.
  A segment starting with `__` is also refused. Words like "latest" or
  "developers" pass. It also lists any `.map` under `.next/static`.
- Today's build has 17 routes and none is refused.
- **Tests:** the matcher, the manifest reader, and the script's exit code on
  fake builds: clean, with a debug route, with a test page, with a source map,
  with no build at all.

---

## D33 — Dependabot and Secret Scanning

**Decision:** Dependabot opens dependency updates, CI scans the whole git
history for secrets on every PR, and the CI token can only read the
repository. All of it is free: Dependabot comes with GitHub, and gitleaks is
MIT (Principle 8). Phase 9b, PR 4.

- **Dependabot (`.github/dependabot.yml`):**
  - weekly, Monday;
  - npm from the root lockfile, which covers every workspace, with minor and
    patch updates grouped;
  - GitHub Actions grouped, in `directories: ["/", "/.github/actions/*"]`. The
    docs say `/` covers `.github/workflows` and a root `action.yml` only, and
    that `directories` accepts globs, so the glob adds the local composite
    actions (`supabase-images` uses `actions/cache`). The file passes the
    SchemaStore schema for Dependabot v2;
  - at most one open PR per ecosystem (`open-pull-requests-limit: 1`). They
    are merged between loops, never while a loop PR is open (CLAUDE.md,
    "Forma de trabajo");
  - `pg-boss` is ignored: migration D17 creates its schema for this exact
    version, so an upgrade needs a new migration.
  - `react-native`, `react-native-*`, `expo` and `expo-*` are ignored. Their
    versions are set by the Expo SDK (`apps/mobile`, SDK 52) and move with
    `npx expo install --fix` when the SDK changes, never by Dependabot.
    Dependabot once proposed `react-native` 0.77 → 0.88 inside the
    minor-and-patch group: 0.88 needs React 19.3 (`apps/mobile` has 18.3.1)
    and is outside SDK 52, and CI does not build the mobile app, so it would
    have passed green.
  - Its PRs run the same CI as any other.
- **CI token:** `ci.yml` sets `permissions: contents: read` for `GITHUB_TOKEN`
  at the workflow root. No job needs more: checkout only reads, and the caches
  use the runner's own token.
- **Secret scan:** job "Secret scan" in `ci.yml` runs gitleaks v8.30.1, pinned
  by image digest, over the full history (`fetch-depth: 0`, since a secret
  removed later is still leaked).
  - Its checkout uses `persist-credentials: false`: the gitleaks container
    mounts the checkout, `.git/config` included, and must not see the token.
  - `.gitleaks.toml` keeps the default rules and allows only Supabase local's
    public demo JWTs (issuer `supabase-demo`, the same in every local stack).
  - `npm run check:secrets` runs the same scan locally.
  - The current history (177 commits) is clean. Planted test strings are
    reported: a JWT with a real issuer and a GitHub token.

**Accepted for now:**
- Dependabot does not update the Node base image: the Dockerfiles take it as
  an `ARG` (D17), and `.nvmrc` pins Node. It does not update the gitleaks
  digest or `SUPABASE_CLI_VERSION` in `ci.yml` either. Those move by hand.
- Dependabot's npm updates bump turbo in `package-lock.json` but not
  `ARG TURBO_VERSION` in the two Dockerfiles (D17), and that ARG is the turbo
  `turbo prune` runs. The step "Dockerfiles pin the lockfile's turbo (D17)" of
  the `app` job (`scripts/check-turbo-version.mjs`) fails until the ARG is set
  to the lockfile's version by hand.
- GitHub's own secret scanning and push protection are repository settings,
  outside this repo; turning them on is the owner's call.
- Ignoring `pg-boss` also silences its security updates. It is checked by hand
  with `npm audit` in every phase. The same holds for the Expo-controlled
  packages.

---

## D34 — Middleware Session Check Without a Call to Auth

**Decision:** the middleware validates the session with
`supabase.auth.getClaims()` instead of `getUser()`. Phase 9b, PR 5.

- `getClaims()` checks the JWT's signature against Supabase Auth's public keys
  (ES256, fetched once and cached). It also refreshes an expired session, so
  the cookie still carries the latest JWT from `custom_access_token_hook`. With
  a symmetric key it falls back to `getUser()` by itself.
- `getUser()` asked Auth on every request. Under load that call was the
  busiest service (up to 3.5 cores in the baseline run).
- What changes: a session revoked in Auth keeps passing the middleware until
  its JWT expires (`jwt_expiry` 900 s). That was already the case for every
  direct call to the Data API, which only checks the JWT, and the pages already
  read claims with `getClaims()`.
- **Accepted for now.** Offboarding (Phase 14, roadmap) must cut access at
  once: a per-user revocation mark read in the middleware (cacheable like
  D35) or a shorter `jwt_expiry`, with a test that a terminated user gets a
  401 or a redirect on the next request.
- The middleware no longer reads the claims twice: one `getClaims()` serves the
  session check, the tenant id header (D27), the tenant check (D21) and the MFA
  step.

---

## D35 — Caching per Tenant and per Request

**Decision:** what every page repeats is read once. A few things are cached
across requests, always keyed by the tenant of the verified token; the rest is
cached only within one request. Phase 9b, PR 5.

**Across requests:**
- **Tenant check of the middleware (D21).** `lib/tenant/access-cache.ts`
  remembers an allowed (requested tenant, token tenant) pair for 5 s.
  - Refusals and failed lookups are never cached.
  - A tenant suspended in the database is refused at most 5 s later; its new
    tokens already leave it out (hook, D21).
  - The cache has an `invalidate(tenantId)` meant for the suspension path.
    Today suspension is done by the platform in SQL, and the cache lives in
    each process, so the 5 s TTL is the bound. A shared store (Redis, Phase 23)
    would implement the same interface and make invalidation reach every
    instance.
  - **Pending for Phase 12:** nothing calls `invalidate` yet. The platform
    console calls `tenantAccessCache().invalidate(tenantId)` when it suspends
    a tenant (roadmap, Phase 12).
- **Module flags.** `getModuleFlags` uses `unstable_cache` per tenant (tag
  `tenant:<id>:modules`, 60 s).
  - It reads through `public.tenant_module_flags(uuid)`, a SECURITY DEFINER
    function only `prisma_app` can execute, which returns the 12 flags of one
    tenant. The cache is filled outside the request, so it cannot use the
    user's session.
  - Callers pass the tenant id of the verified token.
  - **Pending for Phase 12:** nothing invalidates the flags yet. The platform
    console calls `revalidateTag(moduleFlagsTag(tenantId))` when it turns a
    module on or off (roadmap, Phase 12). Until then a module turned off stays
    visible for up to 60 s.
- **RBP stays out of any cross-request cache.** D14 and the roadmap require
  that a revoked permission stops working at once. That needs an invalidation
  this design does not have, so the permission context is still read on every
  request.

**Within one request (React `cache()`):**
- `getClaims()`, the server Supabase client (`createClient()`) and the current
  employee row (`getCurrentUser()`). The layout and the page used to read the
  employee row and the module flags twice each.
- The permission context of `can()` (D14) is now one batch transaction of two
  statements: the RLS context with `set_config('role', …)` and one query that
  returns flags, grants and permissions. The interactive transaction it
  replaced had six statements. Under load the interactive version ran out of
  pooled connections (`P2028`, "Unable to start a transaction"). Since D47 it
  is a single statement, `SELECT rbp_context(…)`, with no transaction.

**Tests:**
- unit: cache TTL, keys and invalidation; the middleware remembers an allowed
  tenant but not a refusal; `getClaims()` instead of `getUser()`; one
  transaction for six `can()` calls;
- integration: `tenant_module_flags()` for `prisma_app` only, the same flags
  as the table, no row for an unknown tenant, refused from the Data API.

---

## D36 — Load Test with k6 Against the Docker Stack

**Decision:** `npm run load:test` runs k6 (grafana/k6 v1.8.2, pinned by
digest, open source) against the local Docker stack and gates on page p95 <
500 ms in steady state. Phase 9b, PR 5.

- **Fixture (`load/seed.mjs`, local database only).**
  - Tenant `load-big` has 1,000 employees; `load-a`, `load-b` and `load-c`
    have 50 each.
  - Each tenant gets the demo tenant's RBP structure, job records with a
    manager per 25 employees, goals, feedback and one Auth account per
    employee.
  - Every run first deletes the previous load data.
- **Scenarios (`load/pages.js`).** All run at the same time:
  - 1,000 users in `load-big` and 50 in each of the other three, 1,150 in all;
  - each one signs in once, straight to Supabase Auth, since the login form's
    limits are per IP (D28);
  - then it opens the dashboard, employees (pages and search), org chart,
    performance, skills hub and profile, with a reading pause between pages
    (20–40 s by default, `THINK_MIN` and `THINK_MAX`).
- **Checks:** every page answers 200, and no page shows another tenant's
  employees.
- **Thresholds:** page p95 < 500 ms in steady state, < 1 % failed requests,
  > 99 % checks. Ramp-up, with 1,150 bcrypt sign-ins in two minutes, is
  reported but not gated.
  - **Isolation threshold of the first pass (#81, 2026-11-20, after D54):**
    the isolation check also has a threshold of its own,
    `checks{check:no other tenant's employees}` at `rate==1`. A single page
    that shows another tenant's employees makes k6 exit 99, which fails the
    step "k6 load test, short run (D36)" and the job. Before, only the
    general `checks` threshold (> 99 %) covered it, and that one lets up to
    1 % of failed checks through, isolation ones included.
    - Unchanged: the general `checks` threshold and the number D36 reads,
      the `{ kind:page,phase:steady }` line of that step's log. The new
      threshold is on another metric, `checks`, so the `http_req_duration`
      submetric D36 reads holds the same samples. In k6's summary (seen in
      the local runs below) it adds, under `checks` in the THRESHOLDS
      section, the submetric `{check:no other tenant's employees}` and its
      `rate==1` line; the line D36 reads is in the HTTP section, as before.
    - What the check sees: in the HTML of each page k6 opens (the 8 routes
      of `load/pages.js`), the tags of the load tenants' employees
      (`LoadGrande`, `LoadAlfa`, `LoadBravo`, `LoadCharlie`), except the
      tag of the VU's own tenant. A page that
      does not answer 200 (a redirect to the login, an error) passes it
      unless its body has one of those tags; such a page fails the "page
      200" check, which counts only in the general threshold's 1 %. It is a
      check under load, not the tenant-isolation guarantee, which rests on
      RLS and its tests against the local database.
    - The second pass (D54) runs the same `load/pages.js`, so it has the
      threshold too: a leaked page makes it exit 99, which it reports, and
      its isolation count still fails the job in its own step.
    - The check must keep its exact name: a threshold whose selector matches
      no check gets no sample, and k6 v1.8.2 does not evaluate a submetric
      without samples, so it would pass unseen. With no page at all (every
      sign-in failed) the submetric is empty too; the general `checks`
      threshold fails the run then, since its only samples are failed
      sign-ins.
    - Unit tests (`apps/web/__tests__/scripts/d36-diag.test.ts`) run
      `load/pages.js` with stubbed k6 modules that record one sample per
      check with its `check` tag, and read the selector from the threshold
      itself: one sample per page; one leaked page in 150, with the tag of
      each of the three other tenants in turn, leaves the general threshold
      at 99.67 % (it holds) and fails the new one; both hold with no leak or
      with the VU's own tenant's tag; with every sign-in failing the
      submetric has no sample and the general threshold fails. With
      `main`'s `load/pages.js` the tests of the new threshold fail.
    - Checked locally, not stored in the repo: k6 v1.8.2 (checked against
      the release's checksum) against a stub server whose first page
      carries two other load tenants' tags. With `main`'s `load/pages.js`
      k6 exits 0 (1 failed check of 1,906, so the general `checks` holds);
      with this one it exits 99 (the submetric at 99.89 %, 1 failed of 948;
      the general `checks` at 99.94 % as k6 prints it, 1 of 1,910); without
      the leak it
      exits 0. The submetric's passes and fails equal those of the check in
      `root_group.checks`, and the log keeps the same two lines with
      `kind:page,phase:steady` (the threshold and the HTTP one).

**Results (4 vCPU and 15 GB, with the whole stack and k6 on the same
machine):**

| Run | Users | Pause between pages | Requests/s | Page p95 | Failed |
|-----|-------|---------------------|------------|----------|--------|
| Before this PR | 1,150 | 2–6 s | 41 (saturated) | 60 s (timeouts) | 60 % |
| After, workload model | 1,150 | 20–40 s | 35 | 217 ms (p99 360 ms) | 0 % |
| After, capacity | 180 | 2–6 s | 38 | 209 ms | 0 % |
| After, past capacity | 250 | 2–6 s | 46 (saturated) | 2.25 s | 0 % |

- **The workload model:** 1,000 concurrent users of an HR application open a
  page every 20–40 s (mean 30 s) while they read and fill in forms. With
  2–6 s between pages, the same 1,150 users would ask for about 280 pages/s.
- **The limit is the web process:** one Next.js process renders a page with
  about 20–25 ms of CPU and serves about 40 pages/s at p95 < 500 ms. Postgres
  stayed under 30 % CPU and Auth near idle once D34 was in.
- **Growing beyond that:** more web processes behind a load balancer (replicas
  in Phase 23), not changes in the database.
- **Pages per second with 20 users and no pause, before → after:**
  - performance 34 → 63 (D38);
  - org chart 54 → 66;
  - skills hub 50 → 61;
  - dashboard 45 → 51;
  - employees 38 → 44;
  - profile 44 → 51.
- **CI:** the Docker job runs a short version (75 users, 2–6 s pauses, 40 s at
  full load) so the tooling keeps working and a large regression shows up. The
  full run is local; see `docs/RUNBOOK.md`.
- **Comparing a pull request with `main` (since 9c PR 6b):** a single p95 of
  `main` is a noisy baseline. The same code of D47 gave 75.6 and 116.5 ms in
  two CI runs, and the merge of 6a gave 157.1 ms on `main` after 113.9 ms on
  its pull request.
  - The baseline is the median of the steady-state p95 of the last three CI
    runs on `main`. (Replaced by the noise band below.)
  - A pull request blocks if its steady-state p95 is more than 20 % above that
    median in two runs in a row: a first run above it is confirmed by
    re-running the Docker job once. It also blocks if the p95 passes 500 ms
    (the threshold of the run itself). (The noise band below replaces the
    20 %.)
  - For 6b: 108.5 ms (387e0d8), 96.8 ms (736ee57) and 157.1 ms (e31ae2d), so
    the median is 108.5 ms and the limit 130.2 ms.
  - **Pull requests that cannot change the images (after 9c PR 6c):** when the
    diff against `main` only touches `**/*.md`, `docs/**` or `.claude/**`, k6
    still runs, but the comparison with the median does not block and needs
    no confirming re-run. The run's own p95 < 500 ms threshold still gates.
    - Why those paths: `docs` is in `.dockerignore`; the final stage of both
      Dockerfiles copies only build output from earlier stages (the web's
      `.next/standalone` and `.next/static`, the worker's `dist` and
      production `node_modules`); and no application source reads a Markdown
      file. A unit test checks these three. `.claude/` and the root Markdown
      files do enter the pruner stage (`COPY . .`), but `turbo prune` does not
      carry them on: checked by hand for both images, with no test.
    - Markdown aside, every other path counts, even one that reaches no image:
      `.github/`, `load/`, `scripts/` and `supabase/` can change the k6 run
      itself.
    - Detection: the step "Does the diff change the images? (D36)" of the
      `docker` job, on pull requests only, so only pull request runs carry a
      verdict. It diffs GitHub's merge commit with its first parent (`git
      diff -z --name-only --no-renames HEAD^1 HEAD`), so the list is what the
      merge adds to `main`. A rename also lists the deleted path, and an
      empty diff (the merge equals `main`) counts as not applicable.
    - The step prints "applies" or "not applicable (no changes in the
      images)" in the log and in the job summary; the pull request's
      verification table writes the second as "no aplica (sin cambios en la
      imagen)". If HEAD is not the pull request's merge commit, or `git diff`
      fails, the step fails the job instead of picking an answer.
    - Since D56 the step also writes `applies=true` or `applies=false` to its
      output. `main`'s k6 passes are skipped only on `false`, and D56's ratio
      step checks that verdict against the diff and against the PR's images.
  - **Noise band (after 9c PR 6c, the user's choice among three variants):**
    it replaces the three-run baseline and the 20 % limit above. With them,
    #73 and #74, whose images had the same code as `main`'s, blocked in two
    runs in a row each. Like the rule it replaces, it is applied by hand:
    the main loop reads the steady-state p95 from the `docker` job's log of
    each run and fills the pull request's verification table. No script
    computes the band.
    - Since D56 (2026-11-20) the gate is the A/B ratio with `main` on the
      same runner, computed by `scripts/d56-ab.mjs`. This band stays for five
      `main` runs as "banda global (informativa)", without blocking, and is
      then dropped from the verification table (D56).
    - Since 2026-11-22 the D36 step's pass follows an unmeasured k6 warm-up
      on the same stack (D56, **Warm-up of the PR's stack**). The step, its
      `{ kind:page,phase:steady }` line and its files keep their names, but
      the band now reads a warmed pass, unlike the `main` runs it was built
      from: one more reason it is only informational.
    - Baseline: the median of the steady-state p95 of the last five CI runs
      on `main`.
    - The last five runs: the latest completed CI runs on `main` whose
      `docker` job reached the k6 summary, one per commit (its latest
      attempt). A cancelled run, or one without a k6 result, is skipped and
      the next older one is taken. Merges of docs-only pull requests count,
      since k6 runs on them too.
    - Maximum age: the five valid runs are looked for only among the last
      ten commits of `main` (its first-parent history: the commits `main`
      pointed to, each with its own CI run). With fewer than five, the
      verification table says "sin línea base": the comparison is not
      applied (the 500 ms ceiling still is), and the pull request is not
      merged without the user's explicit exception. If any of the last
      three `main` runs has no k6 result, an investigation of why is due,
      and the phase's next pull request does not open until the
      investigation is open.
      - Since D56, during its transition, "sin línea base" is informational:
        it no longer holds back a merge, since the gate is D56's ratio (D56,
        **Transition**).
    - Limit: the larger of median × 1.2 and median + 2 × MAD of those five
      runs. MAD is the median of the absolute deviations from the median,
      unscaled.
    - Unchanged: a first run above the limit is confirmed by re-running the
      Docker job once, it blocks only if both runs are above, and the run's
      own 500 ms threshold still blocks.
    - The three variants, applied to the ten `main` runs from 94e2020 to
      fbc9be5 and to #73 and #74 as if each were a pull request. The counts
      use nine samples whose baseline is entirely after the level shift
      below: the five `main` runs e31ae2d, ed27e7f, d7aebe3, 9277389 and
      fbc9be5, and the two runs each of #73 and #74.
      - Three runs, +20 %: false block estimated at 44 % (#73 and #74
        blocked); a regression of +50 % blocks 60 % of the time, +100 % 79 %.
      - Five runs, +20 %: 20 % (#74 blocked); +50 % 60 %, +100 % 79 %.
      - Five runs, the larger of the two (chosen): 5 % (neither blocked);
        +50 % 31 %, +100 % 60 %, +150 % always.
      - The figures are for a pull request's two runs. A `main` run has only
        one, so the chance of a block is taken as the chance of one run
        above the limit, squared, with each sample compared with its own
        baseline. For the chosen variant that is 2, 5 and 7 of the nine
        samples above the limit at +0 %, +50 % and +100 %.
    - What CI no longer catches: with the noise band a false block is
      estimated at 5 %, a regression of +50 % blocks 31 % of the time,
      +100 % 60 % of the time and +150 % always. The sample is nine runs, so
      these numbers are an order of magnitude. Smaller regressions are left
      to the full local run (1,150 users, `docs/RUNBOOK.md`), which is made
      at the close of each phase.
      - The two runs of a pull request are taken as independent. They may
        not be (in #73 and #74 the first run was the slower one), so the
        5 % is more likely low than high. The figures also average past
        windows whose limits went from +20 % to +67 %.
      - A slow creep is not caught by the band either, because its baseline
        moves with `main`. Above +50 % of the drift reference it shows as
        "deriva (referencia)"; below that, only the full local run can find
        it.
    - Limit in force when the band was adopted: the last five runs are
      101.08 ms (ed27e7f), 55.37 ms (d7aebe3), 64.21 ms (9277389), 87.29 ms
      (fbc9be5) and 80.36 ms (30eae8c). The median is 80.36 ms and the MAD
      16.15 ms, so median × 1.2 is 96.43 ms, median + 2 × MAD is 112.66 ms,
      and the limit is 112.66 ms (+40 %). It moves with every `main` run.
    - Level shift of +96 %, uninvestigated: the five runs before 94e2020
      have a median of 47.07 ms and the ten from 94e2020 to fbc9be5
      92.04 ms. It came in with 94e2020 (D43, the component library) before
      the comparison with `main` existed, and nobody reviewed it. Since
      a9c4365 (D44) the e2e also runs before k6 on the same stack, so two
      possible causes are mixed: heavier pages from D43, and a stack that
      has just served the e2e.
    - Drift control (informational, never blocks): the reference is
      92.04 ms, the median of the ten runs from 94e2020 to fbc9be5 (the
      sample measured before 30eae8c), so it already includes the
      uninvestigated level shift. The main loop
      reports the five-run median of `main` against it in every pull
      request's verification table. More than 50 % above it (138.06 ms) is
      marked "deriva (referencia)", and the phase's next pull request does
      not open until the investigation is open. The reference changes only
      by the user's
      explicit decision, noted here.
      - Since D56, during its transition, this control is informational:
        it no longer holds back the next pull request (D56,
        **Transition**).
    - `main` above the limit: every pull request's verification table also
      checks the latest `main` run against the band of the five valid runs
      before it. If it is above, the table notes "main sobre el límite" with
      the commit. Two `main` runs in a row above their limits count as
      "deriva (main ×2)", and the phase's next pull request does not open
      until the investigation is open.
      - Since D56, during its transition, "main sobre el límite" and
        "deriva (main ×2)" are informational: they no longer hold back the
        next pull request (D56, **Transition**).
      - The band of a `main` run comes from the five valid runs before it,
        all inside the same ten-commit window. With fewer than five valid
        runs before it, the table says "main sin revisar": informational,
        it does not block. If the latest `main` run has no k6 result, the
        rule above for the last three runs applies.
      - Counted per `main` commit, not per pull request: each pull request
        checks every `main` run that came in since the previous pull
        request's table, each one once. "Two in a row" means two
        consecutive commits of `main`'s first-parent history.
    - "deriva (referencia)" and "deriva (main ×2)" have the same
      consequence.
    - Also:
      - A `main` run above the limit still counts in the baseline. No
        outliers are excluded: removing them would lower the band by hand.
      - An investigation closes when its cause is written in D36, or when
        the user accepts the new level with a decision noted in D36.
      - Since D56, a push to `main` with "regresión ya mergeada" opens an
        investigation under this rule too (D56, **Verdicts**). The first
        one, the push of 65b4fa7 (#85, 2026-11-26), is open: D56,
        **Investigation of 65b4fa7**. Its cause and its closing are written
        here once the user has decided.
        - Closed by the user's decision on 2026-11-26: runner noise, no
          regression of the code. The limit of 1.20 does not change.
        - supabase-js: unchanged on the per-page path.
        - Attempt 1: a slow window of the runner (AMD EPYC 7763, 21:24–21:30)
          that follows the clock, not the code.
        - Attempt 2: within the limit (r 1.1007).
        - Local A/B with no CPU limit: ln r −0.025 (95 % interval −0.152 to
          +0.102), which rules out +0.266.
      - Open investigation, the full run at the close of 9c (2026-11-27):
        the full local run that closes each phase (`docs/RUNBOOK.md`,
        section 9) did not pass. Two runs gave a page p95 in the steady
        state of 1.23 s and 2.0 s, against 217 ms at the close of 9b (D36,
        **Results** table, row "After, workload model") and the ceiling of
        500 ms.
        - Same workload as the D36 model: 1,150 users, 20–40 s between
          pages, about 34.5 requests/s, 0 % failed, 100 % of checks, and no
          page with another tenant's employees.
        - Same spec as the 9b run (4 vCPU, 15 GB, the stack and k6 on the
          same machine): a container with an Intel Xeon at 2.30 GHz, over
          the tree of #88 (42bf78d plus the changes of D57). The CPU model of the 9b run is
          not recorded.
        - Probably related to the level shift of +96 % noted above
          (94e2020, D43). That shift was measured in the short CI run, so the
          link is not measured yet. The comparison that
          confirms or rules it out: the tree of the 9b close (ae25e87)
          against the tree of #88, on the same machine. Done on 2026-11-28,
          against main (2b7a677, the same code as #88): see the result
          below.
        - It is investigated in its own pull request, and Phase 9c does not
          close until then and the user decides (ROADMAP, Fase 9c,
          **Estado**). The first such pull request recorded the result
          below; the investigation is still open.
        - **Result of the investigation (2026-11-28): the code, not the
          machine.** The investigation stays open: the cause is narrowed to
          the code, and its next step is for the user's decision (below).
          - **Method.** All local, in one container: Intel(R) Xeon(R)
            Processor @ 2.10GHz, 4 vCPU, 15.7 GiB (16,473,316 kB), with the
            stack and k6 on it.
            - Each pass is `npm run load:test` with the RUNBOOK's defaults
              (`LOAD_BIG_USERS=1000`, `LOAD_SMALL_USERS=50`,
              `LOAD_VUS=1000`, `SMALL_VUS=50`, `RAMP=2m`, `HOLD=4m`,
              `THINK_MIN=20`, `THINK_MAX=40`), which seeds again before
              every pass. The k6 harness is always main's (`load/` at
              2b7a677): since ae25e87 `load/` changed only its diagnostics,
              tags and thresholds, not the pages or the seed's data.
            - Each tree runs on its own web and worker images, built with
              its own Dockerfiles, and on its own database. On every switch
              of tree: the containers down, `supabase db reset` with that
              tree's migrations, the role passwords, the containers up with
              that tree's compose file, the health check, and one unmeasured
              warm-up with the same values.
            - p95 is the summary's `http_req_duration{kind:page,phase:steady}`.
              Web CPU and memory come from `docker stats` of the web
              container every 10 s: the CPU is the mean, and the memory the
              median, of the samples from 2 min 10 s to 6 min after the pass
              starts (the time at full load).
            - The warm-up before pass 2 overlapped a local lint, typecheck
              and build (01:03–01:05 UTC). It is not measured, and pass 2,
              right after it, is in line with the other B passes.
          - **A/B, ABBA and then BAAB:** A is the tree of the 9b close
            (ae25e87), B is main (2b7a677).

            | Pass | Tree | p95 steady | Requests/s | Failed | Checks | Web CPU | Web memory |
            |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
            | 1 | A | 149 ms | 34.85 | 0 % | 100 % | 80 % | 511 MiB |
            | 2 | B | 6.65 s | 32.61 | 0 % | 100 % | 115 % | 3,426 MiB |
            | 3 | B | 5.66 s | 32.87 | 0 % | 100 % | 115 % | 3,557 MiB |
            | 4 | A | 308 ms | 34.80 | 0 % | 100 % | 91 % | 912 MiB |
            | 5 | B | 7.33 s | 32.38 | 0 % | 100 % | 125 % | 3,607 MiB |
            | 6 | A | 137 ms | 34.84 | 0 % | 100 % | 77 % | 568 MiB |
            | 7 | A | 258 ms | 34.88 | 0 % | 100 % | 106 % | 360 MiB |
            | 8 | B | 7.62 s | 32.26 | 0 % | 100 % | 115 % | 3,564 MiB |

            - Every page answered 200 and none showed another tenant's
              employees (the isolation check at 100 % in all 8). k6 exited 0
              on every A pass and 99 on every B pass (the p95 threshold).
            - The four B passes are all above 5.6 s and the four A passes
              all below 310 ms: a complete separation.
            - ln(B/A) of each adjacent pair: +3.798 (ABBA, A first), +2.911
              (ABBA, B first), +3.981 (BAAB, B first), +3.385 (BAAB, A
              first). Mean **+3.519** (B about 34 times A); SD pooled within
              the two orders of a pair, A first (+3.798, +3.385) and B first
              (+2.911, +3.981), so a position effect stays out of the noise:
              0.574 (2 degrees of freedom), SE 0.287; 95 % interval with
              Student's t (df 2, 4.303): **+2.284 to +4.753**. The ratio is
              not a constant of the code: past the limit the queue grows
              with the time at full load, so it says "much worse", not how
              much.
            - A stays under 500 ms on this machine in all four passes.
          - **Bisection, with the same full run** (one warm-up and two
            measured passes per tree, run once each in commit order, not
            counterbalanced):

            | Tree | p95 steady | Requests/s | Web CPU | Web memory |
            |---|---|---|---|---|
            | e8459db (D42, the parent of 94e2020) | 298 ms; 523 ms | 34.83; 34.81 | 89 %; 104 % | 783; 581 MiB |
            | 94e2020 (D43) | 8.52 s; 9.80 s | 31.86; 30.10 | 117 %; 113 % | 3,613; 2,305 MiB |
            | a9c4365 (D44) | 4.48 s; 7.63 s | 33.53; 32.29 | 117 %; 114 % | 3,635; 2,195 MiB |

            All passes: 0 % failed and 100 % of checks; k6 exited 0 on
            e8459db's first pass and 99 on the rest (p95 over 500 ms).
          - **Separated effects:**
            - **D43 (94e2020)** is where the full run jumps, from 0.3–0.5 s
              to 8.5–9.8 s, and no later tree measured comes back down (D44
              and main stay at 4.5–7.6 s). It tips a margin that was already
              eroded: D42 (e8459db) gave 298 ms and 523 ms, and its second
              pass already failed the 500 ms threshold, against 137–308 ms
              for the 9b close. D40, D41 and D42 were not bisected one by
              one.
            - **D44 (a9c4365)** gave 4.48 s and 7.63 s against D43's 8.52 s
              and 9.80 s: lower in both passes, but within the spread of two
              passes per tree, so an effect of its own is neither shown nor
              ruled out. Its
              "e2e before k6" happens only in the CI `docker` job; the local
              full run has no e2e before it, so that part of D44 can only
              touch the short CI run (the +96 % level shift above mixes it
              with D43). The commits between a9c4365 and main were not
              measured one by one.
            - **The machine** changes how far past the limit the queue goes.
              The same code as main (the tree of #88, whose code is
              2b7a677's) gave 1.23 s and 2.0 s on a container with an Intel
              Xeon at 2.30 GHz (2026-11-27) and 5.7–7.6 s on this one at
              2.10 GHz: one pair of machines, on different days. The 9b
              tree ran only on this machine; at the 9b close D36 measured
              217 ms on a 4 vCPU machine whose CPU model is not recorded.
          - **Mechanism:** the single web process saturates. From D43 on,
            it serves about 30–33.5 requests/s against the 34.8 the workload
            asks for, its CPU stays at 113–125 %, and the queue makes the
            p95 grow to seconds; before D43 it keeps up at 34.8 requests/s
            with 77–106 %.
            - At low load (4 users, 1 s between pages, 2 min after a 1 min
              warm-up, no queue) the median per page is about the same in
              every tree (46–54 ms). The web CPU per request at low load
              (mean CPU over requests per second) is about 34 ms (9b), 42
              ms (D42), 47 ms (D43) and 49 ms (main); a warm-up and a
              measured pass of one tree differ by up to 20 %, so these
              figures only say the cost grew across 9c, not by how much
              each step.
            - The web memory at full load is 0.4–0.9 GiB in the fast passes
              and 2.2–3.6 GiB in the slow ones, and it falls after a major
              collection (94e2020's second pass dropped from 3.6 GiB to
              0.66 GiB 78 s in, with no failed request). Whether that is a
              cause (D43 allocating more, and the garbage collector's CPU)
              or an effect (the queue's requests in flight) is not
              measured.
          - **Which part, not isolated yet.** Candidates from D43's diff:
            `Table` renders every row twice (the table and the cards under
            768 px, one of them hidden), and `/employees` is 6 of the 14
            weights of the page mix; `(protected)/loading.tsx` puts every
            protected page behind a Suspense boundary (streamed response);
            `ToastProvider` wraps the protected layout; and the rewritten
            pages (dashboard, employees, the employee's page, performance)
            with the larger `es-MX` dictionary. Between ae25e87 and 94e2020
            the compose file only gained the D41 demo-login variable, unset
            here.
          - **Status and proposal, for the user's decision:** Phase 9c stays
            with its closing criterion pending (ROADMAP, Fase 9c,
            **Estado**); the limit of 500 ms, the D56 gate and the tests do
            not change.
            1. A pull request of its own, in Phase 9c before it closes,
               that isolates the part: per-route CPU at a fixed arrival
               rate, read from the web container's cgroup `cpu.stat`, on
               e8459db and 94e2020 (and D40–D42), then a 94e2020 build
               without each candidate, and one ABBA full run to confirm.
               Then a pull request that brings the per-page cost back and
               reruns the full run.
            2. Or a decision on the full run's setup (one web process,
               1,150 users at 20–40 s), which this investigation does not
               change.
      - "The last ten commits" are counted when the pull request's
        verification table is filled, that is, when the CI of its head
        finishes.
  - **Diagnostics of the first attempt (D54):** since D54 the `docker` job
    prints "D36-diag:" lines (runner, seconds from the web container turning

    pass after the first, with an artifact per attempt. None of it changes
    what D36 compares.
    - The value read is still the `{ kind:page,phase:steady }` line of the
      step "k6 load test, short run (D36)": the first pass. Since D54 it is
      the only such line in the job's log.
    - The second pass never counts for D36: not as a pull request's run, not
      as the confirming re-run, not as a `main` sample of the baseline, and
      not for the limit. It is informational, and its numbers appear only in
      its "D36-diag:" lines (`second pass`, and `sign-ins` and `requests`
      with `pass=second`) and in the artifact.
      - Since D56 the second pass is also `pr-2` of the A/B ratio, which
        replaces the band as the gate. For the band, informational during
        D56's transition, it still never counts.
    - CSV transition (pending the user's decision): since D54 the first pass
      writes a CSV. The baseline mixes `main` runs without and with the CSV
      until there are five `main` runs with it. During that transition D36
      is reported the same way, with the label "transición CSV".
      - Accepted by the user on 2026-11-19, in #79, so it is no longer
        pending. The label stays until there are five `main` runs with the
        CSV.
  - **Exception for #79 (D54), approved by the user on 2026-11-19:** D36
    blocked #79. Its head 0342b31 was above the limit of 96.43 ms on the
    first attempt and on the re-run, both on AMD EPYC 7763 runners. The user
    approved the merge as an explicit exception, because the block followed
    the runner's hardware and not the code: the images of dd61252 and
    0342b31 hold the same code, and dd61252 gave 54.73 and 61.61 ms on

    warning and its comment, the text one test expects, and docs changed:

    | Head | Attempt | Runner CPU | k6 p95 steady | Against 96.43 ms |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | dd61252 | 1 | Intel(R) Xeon(R) 6975P-C | 54.73 ms | −43.2 % |
    | dd61252 | 2 | Intel(R) Xeon(R) 6975P-C | 61.61 ms | −36.1 % |
    | 0342b31 | 1 | AMD EPYC 7763 64-Core Processor | 98.13 ms | +1.8 %, above |
    | 0342b31 | 2 (re-run) | AMD EPYC 7763 64-Core Processor | 112.8 ms | +17.0 %, above |

    - Runs 44602211249 (dd61252) and 44608857876 (0342b31); the CPU comes
      from their "D36-diag: runner" lines.
    - By the same decision, #79's closing commit, which only adds this

    - It covers #79 only. Any other pull request above the limit follows

## D37 — Prisma Connection Pool

**Decision:** the Prisma pool is set explicitly instead of Prisma's default
(2 × CPUs + 1). Phase 9b, PR 5.

- Docker stack: `connection_limit=20&pool_timeout=10` on `DATABASE_URL`.
  Local Postgres allows 100 connections.
- Hosted (Phase 23): the same parameters through Supavisor in transaction
  mode (`:6543`, `pgbouncer=true`), as `.env.example` shows. Transaction mode
  fits D3 and D35: the RLS context is set with `SET LOCAL` or a local
  `set_config` inside each transaction.
- The local stack does not run Supavisor (it is excluded from `supabase
  start`), so the load test goes straight to Postgres.

---

## D38 — Pagination and Indexes for Large Tenants

**Decision:** no page lists a whole tenant. Long lists are paged, and choosing
one person is a search. Phase 9b, PR 5.

- **Paged, 20 per page:**
  - employees (already);
  - feedback on `/performance` (`?fb=`);
  - ARCO requests (`?page=`).
  - Page numbers are validated with zod.
- **Search instead of a list.**
  - The feedback recipient and the ARCO subject were `<select>`s holding
    every employee (999 in `load-big`). They are now `components/person-search.tsx`
    over `GET /api/colleagues?q=`:
    - zod (2–60 characters);
    - `limitApi()` (D28);
    - the caller's own tenant, active people only, at most 20;
    - the search text quoted in the `or()` filter (D28);
    - read under RLS with the caller's session;
    - the caller left out unless `self=1`, which ARCO uses.
  - `/performance` went from 34 to 63 pages/s.
- **Bounded by the person, kept as they are:** the viewer's own team (direct
  reports), their own skills and goals, the skill catalog and the roles.
- **Not a list:** the org chart draws the tenant's positions as a tree. Loading
  it by level is a product decision for its own phase.
- **Index:** `d38_users_active_by_name (tenant_id, last_name, first_name)
  WHERE deleted_at IS NULL AND status = 'ACTIVE'`. The employee list now reads
  its 20 rows in order instead of every employee of the tenant plus a sort;
  it was used 11,814 times in the final run.
- **Index review with the load data:** 29 of the 199 indexes of `public` were
  used. The unused ones are primary keys and unique constraints, the covering
  indexes of foreign keys (required by the advisors, D25) and modules outside
  the load mix. None is dropped without production statistics. The tables with
  effective dates (`ec_*`) keep their GiST exclusion indexes on (user, date
  range), which also serve lookups by user.
- **Since 9c PR 6c, `/api/colleagues` is a POST.** The search goes in a JSON
  body validated with Zod (`q`, 2 to 60 characters; `self`, a boolean), so it
  never sits in a URL (D27). The rate limit, the session check and the 20
  results are unchanged. After the limiter and the session, in this order:
  - **Content-Type:** anything but `application/json` (a parameter such as
    `charset` is fine) gets 415. A form on another site can only send
    `text/plain`, a form encoding or `multipart`; a `fetch` with JSON from
    another site needs a CORS preflight, and this route never approves one.
  - **Origin:** it must be present, and its host and port must be the
    request's `Host`, the header D21 reads for the tenant. A missing Origin,
    another site, another tenant's subdomain, another port or `null` gets 403
    with `safeError()`: the generic message with the request id. The reason
    (missing or foreign) goes to the logs, without the Origin's value.
    Browsers send Origin on every POST, so PersonSearch and ⌘K are not
    affected. A proxy in front of the app (Phase 23) must keep `Host`, or this
    check must read the forwarded host the proxy sets.
  - **Body:** invalid or not JSON gets 400 with `{ colleagues: [] }` and
    `Cache-Control: private, no-store`.
  - **GET** gets 405 with `Allow: POST`, after spending a token like every
    handler (D28).
  - PersonSearch and ⌘K (D49) show their safe search message for a 400, 403
    or 415, as for any other failure. They never show the route's text, and
    never "no results". The safe 429 message is unchanged.
  - Tested in unit tests (each refusal, its order after the limiter and the
    session, and PersonSearch's message) and in the production image (the
    browser's real Origin passes; GET, `text/plain`, a foreign Origin, another
    subdomain and an invalid body are refused).

---

## D39 — Least-Privilege Tokens in Every Workflow; One Parser for the Static Checks

**Decision:** the two follow-ups of Phase 9b PR 4. Phase 9b, PR 5.

- **`db-remote-sync.yml`:** `permissions: contents: read` at the root, like
  `ci.yml` (D33). Its checkout uses `persist-credentials: false`, since no
  later step runs git; the scripts and the Supabase CLI that follow do not see
  the token.
- **One parser for the static checks.** `__tests__/static/source-exports.ts`
  reads exports with the TypeScript parser and is shared by the rate-limit
  coverage test (D28) and the zod test (D31).
  - `export const x = async (...) =>` actions are checked too, and so are
    route handlers written as `const GET = …`.
  - Each body is only its own.
  - Any export the parser does not recognize in a `"use server"` file fails
    both tests.

---

## D40 — Design Tokens and Theme Base

**Decision:** every color, radius, shadow, font and duration the web app uses
is a semantic token. A preset is data: one file of values per preset, rendered
to CSS variables on the server. Tailwind 4 reads the tokens through `@theme`,
and its default palette is removed, so a component cannot use a literal color.
Phase 9c, PR 1 (D18, D19).

**Three layers:**

1. **Preset values** (`src/lib/theme/presets/clean.ts`): a typed object with
   the light and dark color sets and the shape values (radii, shadows). The
   five presets of Phase 11 (Glass, Soft, Midnight, Editorial, Bento) are five
   more files of the same type. The high-contrast variant is derived from the
   preset (D18 calls it automatic); a preset may override single values.
2. **Theme CSS** (`themeCss(preset)` in `src/lib/theme/`): renders the
   `--theme-*` variables for each mode and contrast, and validates every value
   against a strict format (hex colors, lengths, shadow lists) before it
   reaches CSS. A theme is never a stylesheet (D18), so tenant values in
   Phase 11 go through the same check. The root layout puts it in a `<style>`.
3. **Tailwind contract** (`src/app/globals.css`): `@theme inline` maps each
   utility token to its `--theme-*` variable, and `--color-*: initial` drops
   the default palette. `bg-slate-800` generates no CSS; `bg-surface` does.

**Color tokens.** All are opaque except `overlay`, so every pair has one
contrast ratio to test.

| Group | Tokens (utility) | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Surfaces | `canvas`, `surface`, `surface-muted`, `surface-raised`, `surface-hover`, `surface-strong`, `overlay`, `qr-surface` | Page, cards, subtle sections, inputs and chips, hover, neutral fills (progress tracks), modal backdrop, the light background a QR code needs |
| Text | `fg`, `fg-muted`, `fg-subtle` | Primary, secondary and tertiary text (placeholders included) |
| Lines | `line`, `control` | Decorative dividers and outlines; the boundary of form controls |
| Focus | `focus` | Focus indicator |
| Accent | `accent`, `accent-hover`, `accent-fg`, `accent-subtle`, `accent-line`, `on-accent` | Primary action and active state, its hover, accent text and links, selected background, tinted border, text on the accent |
| States | `success`, `warning`, `danger`, `info`, each with `-hover`, `-fg`, `-subtle`, `-line` and `on-` | The same five roles as the accent, per state |
| Categories | `category-1` … `category-3`, each with `-fg`, `-subtle`, `-line` | Labels that tell categories apart (skill types, ARCO request types) and carry no state |

**Contrast rules**, tested for every pair in light, dark, high-contrast light
and high-contrast dark:
- 4.5:1 for text: `fg`, `fg-muted`, `fg-subtle` and every `-fg` token on every
  surface they are used on; each `-fg` on its `-subtle`; each `on-` on its
  solid color and its hover.
- 3:1 for components: `control` and `focus` against the surfaces; `accent`,
  the states and `surface-strong` as indicators (progress bars, active tabs,
  status dots) against the surfaces. Indicators rest on `canvas`, `surface`,
  `surface-muted` and `surface-raised`; `surface-hover` is transient and only
  carries text, which is tested on it.
- High contrast raises text to 7:1 and boundaries to 4.5:1. The derivation
  (`deriveHighContrast()`) removes the tints (every `-subtle` becomes the
  plain surface), makes muted and subtle text equal to `fg`, turns tinted
  borders into their text color, and gives each solid fill black or white text
  at 7:1. `ensureContrast()` does the adjusting; Phase 11 uses it for tenant
  brand colors.

**Other tokens:**
- Radii: `--radius-xs` … `--radius-2xl` and `full`. Clean keeps Tailwind's
  values.
- Shadows: `--shadow-2xs` … `--shadow-2xl`, per mode (Tailwind's values in light, darker in dark).
- Spacing: Tailwind's 4 px step (`--spacing: 0.25rem`), plus the form spacing
  of D19 as `form-label` (8 px), `form-help` (4 px), `form-field` (20 px) and
  `form-section` (24 px).
- Typography: `--font-sans` is Inter, self-hosted with `next/font/local` (SIL
  Open Font License 1.1, license file next to the font); `--font-mono` is the
  system monospace stack. The type scale is Tailwind's.
- Motion: `--motion-fast`, `--motion-base`, `--motion-slow` and
  `--ease-standard`; under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` every duration is
  0.

**Theme selection, on `<html>`:**
- `data-preset="clean"`.
- `data-theme="system" | "light" | "dark"`, default `system` (follows
  `prefers-color-scheme`).
- `data-contrast="auto" | "more"`, default `auto` (follows
  `prefers-contrast: more`).
- `system` and `auto` are resolved by CSS media queries, so with nothing
  stored the page is right on first paint without any script. A stored choice
  (light, dark or more contrast) is applied by an inline script in `<head>`
  before the first paint. That script carries the request's CSP nonce (D29),
  accepts only the listed values and changes nothing else.

**Components** use only these tokens. A static test fails on Tailwind palette
classes, arbitrary color values, hex colors and opacity modifiers on colors
(a translucent token has no fixed contrast), and on a solid accent or state
background whose text is not its `on-` token.

**Fonts and CSP:** the CSP no longer lists `fonts.googleapis.com` or
`fonts.gstatic.com`; fonts and styles come from `'self'` only, and
`scripts/check-csp.mjs` fails if an external origin appears in `font-src` or
`style-src`.

**Implementation (PR 1 of Phase 9c):**
- `src/lib/theme/`: `tokens.ts` (names and types), `presets/clean.ts` (the
  values), `contrast.ts` (WCAG ratio, pairs, `ensureContrast()`),
  `high-contrast.ts`, `css.ts` (`themeCss()`), `boot-script.ts` and `index.ts`
  (the CSS and attributes the app renders today).
- `src/app/layout.tsx` renders `data-preset`, `data-theme` and `data-contrast`
  on `<html>`, the boot script with the nonce and the theme `<style>`.
  `global-error.tsx` renders its own `<html>` with the same tokens.
- `src/fonts/inter/`: Inter variable, latin subset (U+0000–00FF, which covers
  Spanish), from `@fontsource-variable/inter` 5.3.0, with `OFL.txt` and its
  SHA-256 in a README.
- Migration: 883 palette classes in 30 files, plus the two hex colors of
  `global-error.tsx`. Two components built class names at runtime
  (`bg-${color}-600/20`), which Tailwind cannot see; they now use fixed maps of
  semantic tones. The org chart avatar showed `text-fg` on the accent fill and
  now uses `on-accent`.
- Category labels: skill types and the ARCO types "Acceso" and "Oposición"
  use `category-1` and `category-2`. "Rectificación" and "Cancelación" keep
  the warning and danger tints they had.

**Tests:**
- `__tests__/theme/contrast.test.ts`: the math against reference ratios, the
  pair list, and every preset in light, dark and both high-contrast variants.
  Clean: 121 pairs per mode; the lowest are 4.50:1 (text) and 3.03:1
  (components) in dark, 7.00:1 and 5.09:1 in high contrast.
- `__tests__/theme/theme-css.test.ts`: the selectors for each mode and
  contrast, that `themeCss()` refuses anything but plain colors, lengths and
  shadows (`;}`, `url()` and unknown fonts throw), that `globals.css` maps
  exactly the token list, and that the boot script sets only allowed values
  and survives blocked storage.
- `__tests__/static/no-literal-colors.test.ts`: palette classes, arbitrary
  colors, hex and color functions, opacity modifiers on tokens, class names
  built at runtime, unknown tokens, radii or shadows outside the tokens, and
  the wrong text on a solid fill.
- `check-csp` over the production image's `/login`, and a browser run over the
  same image: 8 pages in light, dark, high-contrast light and high-contrast
  dark with no CSP violation and no console error, Inter loaded from the app,
  and a stored choice already applied when `<body>` is created.

**Accepted for now:**
- With `data-theme="system"` as the default, people whose OS is light see the
  light Clean theme; before this PR the app was always dark.
- There is no theme control in the UI yet. The user preferences of Phase 11
  write the two storage keys the boot script reads (`scalia.theme`,
  `scalia.contrast`).
- The email templates (`packages/email`) keep literal colors: email clients do
  not read CSS variables. Their brand values come from D20.

---

## D41 — Responsive Layout and Navigation

**Decision:** one application shell for every protected page, with the
navigation of D19 at three widths, all fed by the same filtered list of
modules. Phase 9c, PR 2. ⌘K is not part of it.

**Shell (`(protected)/layout.tsx`):**
- Landmarks `header`, `nav` and `main`, and a "Saltar al contenido" link as
  the first focusable element (WCAG 2.4.1).
- Padding that follows the width; no horizontal scroll at 320 px (WCAG
  1.4.10), measured at 320, 360, 768 and 1280 px.

**Navigation by width** (Tailwind `md` = 768 px, `xl` = 1280 px, the D19
reference widths):
- 1280 px and up: full sidebar that can be collapsed. The state lives in a
  cookie the server reads, so the first paint is right with no script. The
  cookie only accepts `1` (collapsed) or `0`; any other value means expanded.
  It is `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, and `Secure` when the app runs on https.
- 768 to 1279 px (1024–1279 included): icon rail. Each icon has its
  accessible name from `aria-label` or visually hidden text; the tooltip
  appears on hover and on keyboard focus but never names the link.
- Under 768 px: bottom dock with at most 5 items, each with icon and text.
  With more than 5 modules, the fifth is "Más", which opens an accessible
  dialog (`role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, focus trapped, Esc closes and focus
  returns to "Más"). The dock respects `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`, and
  `main` keeps bottom padding so the dock never covers content or the focused
  element (WCAG 2.4.11).
- In all three: the same `NAV_ITEMS` through the same `filterNavByFlags`
  (module flags, D12, and RBP, D14); `aria-current="page"` on the active item;
  44 px touch targets; "Cerrar sesión" always reachable.
- The menu only hides what the user cannot open. Each page keeps its own
  server guard (`isModuleRouteBlocked` and `can()`), and a test opens hidden
  routes to show they stay blocked whatever the menu shows.

**Also in this PR (from the 9c accessibility and login items):**
- `<html lang="es-MX">` (WCAG 3.1.1).
- The demo credentials leave the code. The login page (a server component)
  shows a hint only when the server variable `SCALIA_DEMO_LOGIN_HINT` holds
  one, never a `NEXT_PUBLIC_` variable. It is ignored when `NODE_ENV` is
  `production` and `APP_BASE_URL` is not a local host (`localhost`,
  `*.localhost`, `127.0.0.1`). `check:prod-build` fails if the client bundle
  contains `Demo1234` or the demo email, and the Docker job checks that the
  image's `/login` shows no hint. The seed and the migrations are unchanged;
  removing the demo data is already an entry requirement of Phase 23.

**Left for PR 3:** the interface texts still in English and the dates, which
will follow the tenant's time zone (`platform.timezone`).

**Implementation:**
- `src/lib/navigation/shell.ts` (cookie, dock split, active link),
  `focus-trap.ts`, and `nav-config.ts` with a short label per module for the
  dock. Long labels carry soft hyphens (`Organi\u00ADgrama`): at 320 px they
  break onto a second line with a hyphen instead of being cut off.
- `src/components/navigation/app-navigation.tsx` (sidebar, rail, dock and the
  "Más" dialog) and `nav-icons.tsx`; the old `components/sidebar.tsx` is gone.
- The header's sign-out button under 768 px opens its tooltip below and to
  the left. Opened to the right it pushed a 320 px page 87 px wider even while
  invisible.
- `src/lib/auth/demo-hint.ts` (`server-only`); `login/page.tsx` passes the
  hint to the form. `docker-compose.yml` forwards an empty
  `SCALIA_DEMO_LOGIN_HINT` unless `.env.docker` sets it.
- `/skills-hub` now refuses a user without `tih.employee_skills` (view), like
  every other module page. The menu already hid it, but the page opened. Every
  role of the demo RBP holds that permission, so nobody loses access today.
- `jsdom` 30.1.3 (MIT) is a dev dependency for the DOM tests, and Vitest
  compiles JSX for them.

**Tests:**
- `__tests__/navigation/shell.test.ts`: the cookie with valid and invalid
  values, the dock split, the dock labels, the active link and the demo hint
  (including production with a host that is not local).
- `__tests__/navigation/navigation-dom.test.ts` (jsdom): the "Más" dialog
  with the keyboard (initial focus, Tab and Shift+Tab wrap, Esc and the close
  button return the focus to "Más"), rail names and tooltips, Esc on a
  tooltip, the sidebar cookie, the header tooltip placement, and the same
  links in the three variants.
- `__tests__/navigation/hidden-routes-guarded.test.ts`: every module the menu
  can hide, opened with its module off and without its permission, is hidden
  by the menu and refused by the server.
- `check:prod-build` fails on the demo password or email in `.next/static`;
  the Docker job checks the image's `/login`.
- In the production image, with a browser: 6 pages × 4 widths (320, 360,
  768, 1280) × light and dark with no horizontal scroll, the dock, rail or
  sidebar at the right width, every navigation target at least 44 × 44 px,
  no CSP violation and no console error; the skip link first; focus never
  under the dock or header while tabbing through a page at 360 px; the "Más"
  dialog with the keyboard; a rail tooltip on focus; and the collapsed
  sidebar rendered by the server after a reload. The demo hint shows only
  when the local stack sets the variable.

**Accepted for now:**
- Dock labels are 12 px, below the 16 px body text of D19; the full name is
  in the text and the dialog.
- The module labels keep their codes ("Inicio (BizX)"); they change with the
  texts of PR 3.
- Under 768 px, sign-out sits in the header, since the dock keeps its five
  places for modules.

## D42 — Spanish Interface Texts and Dates in the Tenant's Time Zone

**Decision:** the texts of the screens this PR touches and the labels of
every database enum live in one es-MX messages module, with no i18n library.
Dates are formatted on the server with `Intl.DateTimeFormat("es-MX")`:
moments in the tenant's time zone, calendar dates with no time zone
conversion. Phase 9c, PR 3.

**Texts (`src/lib/i18n/es-MX.ts`):**
- One typed `as const` object grouped by screen, plus the labels of every
  enum created in `supabase/migrations`, keyed by the enum's name.
- It holds the texts of the screens this PR touches: login, the app
  metadata, dashboard, profile, employee list and detail (with the history
  component), org chart and MFA enrollment. Screens already in Spanish keep
  their texts in the component and move into the module when they are next
  touched (ROADMAP): performance, Talent Hub, roles and permissions, ARCO,
  Admin Center, MFA verification and the employee change dialogs.
- Terms: "Iniciar sesión", "Correo electrónico", "Contraseña",
  "Calificación de desempeño", "Calificación de potencial", "Número de
  empleado", "Jefe directo", "Por contratar" and "App de autenticación" (the
  name of the MFA factor). Product names stay (Authy, 1Password).
- Enum values stored in the database (`ACTIVE`, `ON_TRACK`…) do not change;
  they are translated only when shown, with `enumLabel()`. `ON_LEAVE` is
  "Con permiso" everywhere. The label maps repeated in five components
  (dashboard, employee list and detail, performance, ARCO) give way to the
  module, and so do the feedback form's options; the color maps stay keyed
  by value.
- Goal states agree with the interface's noun, "objetivo": "Sin iniciar",
  "En curso", "En riesgo", "Completado", "Cancelado" (the proposal's table
  said "Completada" and "Cancelada"). `MANAGER_ONLY` feedback is "Solo jefe
  directo", in line with "Jefe directo".

**Dates (`src/lib/format/dates.ts`):** two explicit functions, chosen by the
caller. Neither guesses from the column name or the shape of the value.
- `formatDateOnly(value)` for columns that hold a calendar date. It reads the
  `YYYY-MM-DD` of the value and formats it in UTC, so "31 dic 2026" is the
  same in every zone. It accepts a `date` (`2026-12-31`) and the midnight-UTC
  `timestamptz` that some date-only columns hold today
  (`2026-12-31T00:00:00+00:00`); anything else is an error. The exact list of
  columns is in a comment in the file.
- `formatInstant(value, timeZone)` for moments, in es-MX with a 12-hour clock
  and the tenant's zone: "5 nov 2026, 11:30 p.m.".
- The date-only `timestamptz` columns (`period_start`, `period_end` and
  `due_date` of goals, `users.hire_date`) hold midnight UTC because the
  Postgres sessions run in UTC: `'2026-01-01'::timestamptz` is
  `2026-01-01T00:00:00+00:00`. Converted to America/Mexico_City, a period
  that starts on 1 January would show "31 dic 2025". `formatDateOnly`
  depends on that UTC session, and an integration test checks
  `show timezone` = `UTC` and fails with a clear message if it changes.
  Converting those columns to `date` is planned with expand/contract (D22):
  goals in Phase 16, `hire_date` in Phase 14.
- All formatting runs on the server and client components receive strings,
  so the text is the same in every browser time zone and hydration cannot
  differ from the server render. "Overdue" (ARCO) and the default "today" of
  a form are computed on the server in the tenant's zone.

**Tenant time zone:**
- `getTenantTimeZone(tenantId)` calls `public.tenant_time_zone()` as
  `prisma_app`, validates the result with Zod and Intl, and falls back to
  `America/Mexico_City` when it is null or not a valid zone. It is cached per
  tenant under `tenantTimeZoneTag(id)`, like the module flags (D35); the
  Admin Center of Phase 11 revalidates that tag when the zone is edited. The
  tenant id always comes from the server (the verified token or the
  subdomain), never from user input.
- New migration, `public.tenant_time_zone(p_tenant_id uuid) RETURNS text`:
  `LANGUAGE sql`, `STABLE`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path = ''`. It
  returns only `value_json #>> '{}'` of the `platform.timezone` key of that
  tenant: no other key, no key parameter, no row. Execute is revoked from
  PUBLIC, `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` and granted only to
  `prisma_app`, as with `tenant_module_flags` (D35). It exists because the
  SELECT policy of `system_configurations` lets only tenant admins read it,
  so an employee's session cannot, and the cache is filled outside any
  request. `system_configurations` has no `deleted_at`. The proposal's "no
  migrations" meant no column, policy or seed change, and none of those
  change.
- `create-tenant.ts` writes `platform.timezone` (`America/Mexico_City`) and
  `platform.locale` (`es`) for every new tenant. Until now only the demo
  tenant had them. This is code, not a migration.

**Implementation:**
- `src/lib/i18n/es-MX.ts` (`messages`, `ENUM_LABELS`, `enumLabel()`),
  `src/lib/format/dates.ts` (`formatDateOnly`, `formatInstant`,
  `calendarDateIn`, `canonicalTimeZone`), `src/lib/tenant/get-tenant-time-zone.ts`
  (`getTenantTimeZone`, `readTenantTimeZone`, `getSessionTimeZone`,
  `tenantTimeZoneTag`) and `src/lib/tenant/platform-defaults.ts`.
- Migration `20261106030000_d42_tenant_time_zone.sql`; `create-tenant.ts`
  inserts both keys in the same transaction as the rest of the tenant.
- Server pages format and pass text: performance (`dueLabel`,
  `createdLabel`), ARCO (`dueLabel`, `createdLabel`, `completedLabel`,
  `isOverdue`), the employee detail (history periods and the tenant's
  "today" for the change dialogs), profile and Admin Center (audit times).
- ARCO "overdue" now means the due date has passed in the tenant's zone;
  before, a request became overdue at 00:00 UTC of its due date.
- `src/lib/supabase/mfa.ts` (`enrollTotp`) also named the factor
  "Authenticator App" and now uses the module. Nothing imports it; it stays.

**Tests:**
- `__tests__/static/no-english-ui-text.test.ts`: walks every source file
  with the TypeScript parser and reads only what a user can see (JSX text,
  the `label`, `placeholder`, `title`, `alt` and `aria-label` attributes,
  visible properties such as `label`, `title`, `description`,
  `friendlyName` or `error`, and every string of the messages module). It
  fails on the English texts that were on screen ("Sign In", "Password",
  "ACTIVE", "ON TRACK", "Performance Score", "Employee ID", "To Be Hired",
  "Authenticator App"…). Negative controls: it finds each of them in a
  fixture, and does not count `.eq("status", "ACTIVE")`, a comparison, an
  `<option value>` or "1Password".
- `__tests__/static/no-client-date-formatting.test.ts`: a `"use client"` file
  fails on `toLocale*String`, `toISOString`, `Intl.DateTimeFormat`,
  `new Date()` or an import of `lib/format/dates`, with a negative control.
- `__tests__/i18n/enum-labels.test.ts`: reads every `CREATE TYPE … AS ENUM`
  and `ADD VALUE` in public from the migrations (15 enums) and requires
  exactly those values to have a label.
- `__tests__/format/dates.test.ts` and `__tests__/tenant/tenant-time-zone.test.ts`:
  moments near midnight in two zones, daylight saving time, calendar dates
  under six process zones, values that are refused instead of guessed,
  invalid zones and the fallback, the cache key, tag and revalidation.
- Integration, against the local database: `tenant-time-zone.test.ts`
  (`prisma_app` gets the demo tenant's zone; anon, an employee and a tenant
  admin get 42501 from the Data API; two tenants each get their own; a
  tenant without the key or an unknown id gets null and the fallback; a
  stored value that is not a zone falls back; the function's definition and
  privileges), `dates-utc-session.test.ts` (`SHOW timezone` is UTC for the
  migration role and `prisma_app`, and a goal written through the Data API
  comes back as `2026-01-01T00:00:00+00:00`, "1 ene 2026"; it fails with an
  explicit message if a session zone changes) and `create-tenant.test.ts`
  (a new tenant has both keys and `getTenantTimeZone()` needs no fallback).
- In the production image, with the browser in America/Mexico_City and in
  Asia/Tokyo: the same dates in both on dashboard, performance, profile,
  employee list and detail, org chart, ARCO and Admin Center; no console
  error (hydration included), no CSP violation, and no English text from the
  list or raw ISO date on any page. Screenshots of login, dashboard and
  performance in light and dark at 360 and 1280 px.

**Accepted for now:**
- The module labels of the navigation keep their codes ("Inicio (BizX)",
  "Talent Hub (TIH)", "Admin Center"). D41 left them for this PR, but the
  approved proposal did not include them; they need a decision of their own.
- "Feedback", "Coaching" and "9-Box" stay: they are the terms HR uses in
  Spanish.
