## D45 — A Failed Query Is Not "No Data"

**Decision:** extends D27. A Supabase query that fails is never shown as an
empty result. The section it feeds shows the error (generic text and request
id, through `safeError()`), or the whole page does when it is the page's main
query. A static test with the TypeScript parser fails when a query's `error`
is not read, in pages, layouts, Server Actions, HTTP routes and the libraries
they call. Correction PR between PR 5 and PR 6 of Phase 9c.

**The bug that started it:**
- The employee detail showed "Sin información laboral registrada" to
  everyone, the admin included (found by the D44 screenshots).
- Its job query embedded `business_units(name)` and `legal_entities(name)` in
  `ec_job_information`, which has no foreign key to either table. PostgREST
  answered PGRST200, `data` was null, and the page read only `data`.
- A failed PostgREST query resolves with `{ data: null, error }`; it does not
  throw. Reading only `data` turns every failure into an empty state. The same
  pattern was in about 70 places.

**Business unit and legal entity:**
- They come from the department of each job record:
  `departments(name, business_units(name), legal_entities(name))`
  (`lib/employees/job-information.ts`, `JOB_INFORMATION_SELECT` and
  `jobOrganization()`), for the current record and the history. No migration.
- The department's own business unit and legal entity are read, so the
  history shows today's values of a past record's department. Whether the two
  fields move into the job record, as in SuccessFactors, is decided in Phase 14
  (ROADMAP).

**The rule (`lib/errors/query-error.ts`, `components/ui/load-error.tsx`):**
- A page with independent queries shows the error per section; the other
  sections still render. `queryErrorMessage(event, error)` returns the
  message, or null; `firstError()` picks the first error of the queries that
  feed one section; `LoadError` shows it with `role="alert"`.
- The whole page goes to error (`PageLoadError`: the page's heading and the
  message) only when its main query fails: the record of the employee detail,
  the own profile on Mi perfil and the dashboard, the employees list, the
  positions of the org chart and the ARCO requests.
- Counts and headings that depend on a failed list do not claim zero: the
  roles, groups, goals and feedback headings drop their count, the stats that
  come from a failed list are not shown, and the employee header does not say
  "Sin puesto" when the job query failed.
- Per page:
  - Employee detail: page error for the employee; sections for personal
    data, job (current, history and manager) and compensation.
  - Employees list: page error for the list and for the direct reports,
    without which the list cannot tell which rows open a profile.
  - Dashboard: page error for the profile; sections for the tenant KPIs and
    the goals.
  - Mi perfil: page error for the profile; the manager is a field of the
    personal data section; feedback is its own section.
  - Desempeño: goals and feedback per tab; the stats come from the goals.
  - Habilidades: radar (position, classification, required skills, own
    skills), catalog (ontology, own skills) and team (direct reports, their
    names and skills) per tab, with their stats.
  - Administración: stats, system configuration, audit log and rate limits
    per block. The rate limits show no defaults when the tenant's own values
    cannot be read: the admin would save over them.
  - Gestión de roles: roles and groups per list.
  - Organigrama and Derechos ARCO: page error.

**`maybeSingle()` only where "no record" is a business case.** `.single()`
answers an error (PGRST116) when there is no row; that error was being read as
"no row" by code that ignored it. Every query that changed:
- `getCurrentUser()`: an account without an employee row (D35, null).
- Employee detail: the employee (no row is a 404: it does not exist or RLS
  hides it); the current personal, job and compensation records (an employee
  may have none yet); the manager (out of the caller's RLS view, shown "—").
- Dashboard and Mi perfil: the profile (no employee row says so); the manager
  on Mi perfil (out of view, "Sin jefe").
- Habilidades: the position and its job classification (none, or not
  readable, means no required skills).
- "Tomar Acción": the employee's name (not visible is "not found").
- Server Actions: the employee row of the actor in the employee actions; the
  ARCO request being processed and the skill being updated ("not found"); the
  membership in `switchTenant` (a refusal) and the settings row in
  `revokeMembership` (a person may have none).
- LMS: the tenant's map in `/lms/launch` and `provisionTenantLms()` (not yet
  provisioned), in `syncLmsUsers()` (not provisioned) and the user's map in
  `getEmployeeCompletions()` (no Moodle account, no completions).
- `.single()` stays after an insert that returns the new row, where a missing
  row is an error.

**The current user (`lib/auth/current-user.ts`):**
- A failed read throws `CurrentUserUnavailable`, which carries safeError()'s
  message; null still means no employee row.
- The protected layout catches it and renders a shell without menu or page:
  the header with "Cerrar sesión", "Algo salió mal" and the message with the
  request id. The person is not sent to the login as if there were no
  session. The Admin Center layout renders nothing in that case (the
  protected layout already shows it).
- Next.js renders the page in parallel with the layout; the page gets the
  same cached rejection and rethrows it. The user never sees it (the layout
  does not render the page), and `onRequestError` skips
  `CurrentUserUnavailable`, already logged by safeError() as
  `auth.current_user` with the same request id.
- Server Actions use `currentUserOrError()`, which returns the message
  instead of throwing: performance, Administración, ARCO and skills actions
  return it; the MFA and login audit helpers skip the audit (the read failure
  is already logged). `/api/colleagues` answers 500 "unavailable", not 401.

**"Tomar Acción":**
- Its six lists were read from the browser with the anon client, and a failed
  list was an empty select. They now come from the Server Action
  `loadActionOptions()`: zod (`z.guid()`, the seed's ids are not RFC 4122),
  `limitRead()` (a token of the api bucket, so reads do not spend the person's
  writes), `can(ec.manage_employees, edit)` on that employee, and safeError().
- If a list fails, the dialog shows the error with the request id and its
  submit buttons are disabled; the next open tries again.

**Routes and libraries:**
- `/lms/launch` reads the cohort before creating the Moodle account. If the
  read fails it answers 500 with a safe error and launches nothing, so no
  account is left outside its cohort and the course is not opened as if the
  person had been enrolled.
- `syncLmsUsers()` ends with an error when the tenant map, the employees or
  the existing maps cannot be read (without the maps it would create every
  account again), and a failed map insert is that user's error.
  `provisionTenantLms()` throws when it cannot tell whether the tenant is
  provisioned, instead of creating a second category and cohort.
- `revokeMembership()` still signs the person out after revoking (the
  membership is gone), and logs a failed read or update of the active tenant.
- `tenantAccessAllowed()` (middleware, D21) still refuses on a failed lookup
  with the same 404, and now logs it with the request id.

**Static test (`__tests__/static/query-errors.ts` and `.test.ts`):**
- A query is a call chain that starts at `.from()` or `.rpc()` (not
  `Array.from`). It is checked where it is awaited: directly, through a
  variable holding the builder (refined with `query = query.eq()`), inside
  `await Promise.all([…])`, or as a branch of a conditional.
- The result must be bound with `error` (or to a name whose `.error` is read)
  and that error must be read again in the same function. Otherwise it
  reports: error not destructured, destructured and never read, result thrown
  away, returned unchecked, awaited inside another expression, a
  `Promise.all` result not bound one by one, or a builder never awaited.
- It runs over every file in `src`, and checks that pages, layouts, Server
  Actions and routes are among them. Negative controls give one reason each
  for a page, a Server Action and a route, and a positive control covers every
  pattern the code uses.

**Tests:**
- Unit: `getCurrentUser()` (null without session or row, the error with
  request id and its JSON line, `currentUserOrError()`); `/api/colleagues`
  with the row unavailable; `/lms/launch` with a failed cohort read (500, no
  Moodle account, no launch, no database text); `onRequestError` skipping
  `CurrentUserUnavailable`. The Server Action tests now mock the employee row
  as `getCurrentUser()` reads it.
- Integration against the local database
  (`__tests__/integration/employee-job-information.test.ts`): the old embed
  answers PGRST200; an admin reads an employee's job with business unit and
  legal entity, and the rendered detail page shows them; with the job query
  failing for real (PGRST200), the page shows the error with the request id,
  not "Sin información laboral registrada", the personal section still
  renders, and the JSON line carries the request id and the code; an employee
  reads their own job with both fields under RLS, and another tenant's job
  gives no rows. The two page tests fail against the page before this change.
- e2e (`employee-detail.spec.ts`): for the employee and the admin, the job
  section of Gabriela Morales shows Puesto, Departamento, Unidad de negocio
  and Entidad legal with values, without the empty state or an error.
- Seen in the browser against the local production build, revoking SELECT
  from `authenticated` on one table at a time (local only, restored after):
  the job section's error with the other sections intact; the layout's error
  page with no menu and no redirect; the rate limits' error without defaults
  or "Guardar"; and "Tomar Acción" with the error and its buttons disabled.

**RLS checked before writing the employee test:** `ec_job_information` is
readable by every member of the tenant (`tenant_id = requesting_tenant_id()`),
so an employee reads their own job; nothing changed in the policies.

**Accepted for now:**
- Next.js still prints its own non-JSON line for the page's rethrown
  `CurrentUserUnavailable` (as in D27); it carries the generic message only.
- Revoking SELECT on `ec_job_information` itself also stops `can()`, whose
  SQL reads that table, so that failure reaches `error.tsx` before the job
  query runs. The section error was checked through an embedded table
  (`job_classifications`).
- The employee detail renders two "Tomar Acción" buttons, the menu's and the
  dialog's own, and a choice in the menu does not open the dialog by itself.
  Seen while checking this PR and left as it was.
- The employee-change, ARCO and manager-assessment actions validate the
  employee id with `z.string().uuid()`, which rejects the seed's ids
  (`e0000000-…`); ids from `gen_random_uuid()` pass. Seen while checking this
  PR, not changed.

---

## D46 — No Hard Delete from the Data API; Ids Validated with `z.guid()`

**Decision:** anon and authenticated cannot delete rows anywhere in `public`:
no DELETE privilege, no PERMISSIVE DELETE policy and no default privilege that
would give it to a new relation. Every id in Server Actions and routes is
validated with `z.guid()`, which accepts the seed's ids. Security PR between
PR 5 and PR 6 of Phase 9c, from the findings of D45; the read side (RLS by RBP
population) is D47.

**What was there (`supabase/migrations/20261109010000_d46_no_hard_delete.sql`):**
- Fifteen PERMISSIVE DELETE policies from the first migrations allowed a delete
  inside the caller's tenant, for the role `public` (anon and authenticated):
  `business_rules`, `continuous_feedbacks`, `departments`,
  `job_classifications`, `job_families`, `job_informations`, `job_roles`,
  `object_definitions`, `performance_goals`, `positions`,
  `succession_nominees`, `succession_plans`, `talent_pool_entries`,
  `talent_pools` and `users`.
- Each was neutralized by a RESTRICTIVE `no_hard_delete_authenticated`
  policy (`USING false`), and anon has no tenant, so nothing could be deleted.
  But dropping one RESTRICTIVE policy would have opened its table.
- Supabase's default grants gave DELETE to anon and authenticated on 36 of the
  39 tables, and the default privileges of `postgres` in `public` kept giving
  it to every new table (D25 removed TRUNCATE, TRIGGER, REFERENCES and
  MAINTAIN, not DELETE).
- No screen deletes: there is no `.delete()` of Supabase or Prisma in the app,
  the worker or the packages. Only the operator scripts delete
  (`restore-tenant`, `e2e-accounts`), connected as `postgres`. The two SQL
  functions that delete (`rate_limit_consume`, `purge_expired_rate_limits`)
  are SECURITY DEFINER, and neither anon nor authenticated may execute them.
  Nothing had to move to soft delete.

**The change:**
- The fifteen PERMISSIVE policies are dropped. The RESTRICTIVE `false` ones
  stay as a second lock: they can only take rows away.
- `REVOKE DELETE ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public FROM anon, authenticated`
  (tables, partitioned tables, views, materialized views and foreign tables),
  and the same on the default privileges of `postgres` in `public`.
- `supabase_admin`'s own default privileges still grant DELETE. They apply only
  to relations `supabase_admin` creates, a migration (run as `postgres`)
  cannot change them on the hosted project, and the catalog test below checks
  every relation whatever its owner.
- A delete from the Data API now gets 42501 instead of an empty result.

**Ids (`z.guid()`):**
- `z.uuid()` and `z.string().uuid()` require an RFC 4122 version and variant.
  The seed's ids (`e0000000-…` employees, `5c000000-…` skills, `aa100000-…`
  job roles) have neither, so on demo data the employee changes ("Tomar
  Acción"), ARCO and the skills actions ("Evaluar") answered "Invalid UUID"
  and saved nothing.
- The nine validations (employee changes, ARCO, five in the skills actions and
  MFA's `factorId`) use `z.guid()`. Supabase Auth's factor ids are v4 and pass
  either way; the rule has no exceptions.

**Tests:**
- `__tests__/integration/no-hard-delete.test.ts`, against the catalog: it
  fails if anon or authenticated hold DELETE on any relation of `public`
  (directly or through PUBLIC), if a PERMISSIVE DELETE or ALL policy applies to
  them, or if the default privileges of `postgres` give it back. Negative
  control: inside a transaction that is rolled back, a table with a DELETE
  grant, a permissive policy and a default privilege are all reported, and a
  RESTRICTIVE `false` policy is not. It also checks that a new table and view
  start without DELETE, that the fifteen tables keep their RESTRICTIVE lock,
  and that an admin's delete through the Data API gets 42501 with the row
  intact.
- Six existing checks that tried to delete (in `db-privileges`,
  `tih-rls-integration`, `rate-limiting`, `tenants-policies` and
  `rls-isolation`) accepted "refused or no rows"; they now expect 42501. None
  was removed.
- `__tests__/static/no-uuid-validation.test.ts`, with the TypeScript parser:
  it fails on a call named `uuid`, `uuidv4`, `uuidv6` or `uuidv7` whose chain
  starts at a name imported from `zod`, in every file of `src`. Negative
  controls cover `z.uuid()`, `z.string().uuid()`, `z.string().trim().uuid()`,
  `z.uuidv4()`, a namespace import and a renamed import; positive controls
  cover `z.guid()`, `crypto.randomUUID()` and uuid calls that are not zod.
- e2e (`save-demo-data.spec.ts`), on the seed's data:
  - The admin saves a job change with "Tomar Acción" for Patricia Castro
    (e13), keeping her title and department, and after a reload her job
    section shows the new reason.
  - The employee rates herself with "Evaluar" on a skill the setup adds with a
    seed-shaped id, and after a reload the catalog shows her level.
  - Both fail against the previous validations.
- `scripts/e2e-accounts.mjs` undoes those writes after the run, as `postgres`
  and with the same local guard: the job changes marked `[e2e]` go and the
  record each one closed is current again; the skill goes with its
  assessments. It fails if anything is left, and it runs before the setup too,
  for a run that did not finish. The audit entries of those writes stay, like
  the logins of every run.

**Found by the new e2e (the zoom check, which runs after it):**
- Habilidades: a long self-assessed skill name scrolled the page sideways
  (81 px) at 1280 px with the root font at 200 %. The "additional skills" list
  now uses columns with a rem minimum and names that wrap, like the other
  grids of D44.
- Administración: the zoom check took the lines of an audit diff below its
  `<pre>` box (`overflow: auto`, so they scroll into view) for text drawn over
  the next entry. It now clips text by its own element's box when that box
  scrolls, as its description already said; a control's own box changes
  nothing.

## D47 — Reads by RBP Population

**Decision:** personal, job, pay, talent and RBP data are read through the
same rule as `can()`: one's own rows, plus the population the role grants
give for the permission that guards the data (D14). Holding an
`is_admin_role` role, or the `owner` app_role, no longer opens them. Second
security PR between PR 5 and PR 6 of Phase 9c, after D46.

**What was there:**
- `ec_job_information`, `group_members`, `role_grants` and `lms_user_map`:
  readable by the whole tenant. Anyone could read every job record (title,
  manager, department), who sits in which group and which roles each group
  holds, so who the administrators are.
- `ec_compensation`, `ec_personal_information` and `employee_skills`: own,
  any `is_tenant_admin()` (any `is_admin_role` grant, whatever its keys) or the
  manager of the current job record.
- `performance_goals`, `continuous_feedbacks` (author or subject),
  `arco_requests` (subject) and `audit_logs`: own or any `is_tenant_admin()`;
  an admin read PRIVATE feedback too.
- `users`: every column, `performance_score` and `potential_score` included,
  for the whole tenant.
- `job_informations`, `succession_nominees` and `talent_pool_entries`:
  readable by the tenant, with no screen.

**The rule (`supabase/migrations/20261110010000_d47_rbp_reads.sql`):**
- The population is defined once, in `rbp_population_user_ids(tenant, user,
  population)`: `all` is the tenant, `self` the user, `direct_reports` whoever
  has the user as manager in their current job record (as `can()` read it),
  `same_department` whoever shares the user's non-null department.
  These are two sources: `same_department` reads `users.department_id`,
  while `direct_reports` (and the department the directory and the employee
  detail show) come from the current job record. Phase 14 makes one source of
  truth for the department.
  `rbp_user_ids()` is the union of the populations of the user's grants of
  roles holding the key and action, if the key's module is active
  (`rbp_module_active()`, the same flags as `PERMISSION_MODULE_MAP`). These
  three are internal: no role of the app may execute them.
- The policies call `rbp_visible_user_ids(key, action)` and
  `rbp_has_permission(key, action)`, which answer for whoever runs the query
  (`current_app_user_id()`, `requesting_tenant_id()`). Both are SECURITY
  DEFINER, STABLE, with an empty `search_path`, executable by authenticated.
- `can()` stops reading `role_grants` and `group_members` as authenticated
  (those are now limited to one's own rows). It loads its context with
  `rbp_context(tenant, user)` and checks `direct_reports` and
  `same_department` with `rbp_target_in_population()`, which only `prisma_app`
  may execute (the D42 pattern), so `can()` answers for any user and does not
  depend on the policies it is compared with. `withTenant()` had no other
  caller and is gone.
- `can()` now evaluates the union of the populations that grant a key. Before,
  the first `direct_reports` or `same_department` grant decided alone: a
  manager who was also an HR administrator (`all`) could be refused depending
  on the order of the grants. Unit test in `can.test.ts`.

| Data | Readable by |
|---|---|
| `ec_compensation` | own + `ec.view_compensation` (new key) |
| `ec_personal_information`, `ec_job_information` | own + `ec.view_team_profiles` + `ec.manage_employees` |
| `employee_skills` | own + `tih.employee_skills` |
| `performance_goals` | own + `pmgm.manage_team_goals` (edit) + `pmgm.manage_goal_plans` (edit) |
| `continuous_feedbacks` | author, subject, and the population of `pmgm.view_team_feedback` for the subject unless PRIVATE |
| `arco_requests` | subject + whoever holds `arco.manage` |
| `audit_logs` | whoever holds `admin.view_audit_logs` |
| `group_members`, `lms_user_map` | own + whoever holds `admin.manage_roles` (edit) |
| `role_grants` | the grants of one's own groups + `admin.manage_roles` (edit) |
| `users.performance_score`, `potential_score` | own + `ec.manage_employees`, through `talent_scores()` |
| `job_informations`, `succession_nominees`, `talent_pool_entries` | nobody (RESTRICTIVE `false`) until they have a screen (Phase 18a) |

- `ec_job_information` keeps its tenant policy and gets a RESTRICTIVE one with
  the rule; the other tables had a single SELECT policy, which is altered.
- **Directory:** the employee list showed the title and department of every
  listed person from their job record. It now calls `directory_entries(ids)`
  (SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE, empty `search_path`, filtered by
  `requesting_tenant_id()`): first name, last name, current title and current
  department, nothing else, and only with `ec.view_org_chart`; without it the
  call fails with 42501 rather than answering an empty directory. The org
  chart reads positions, not job records, and did not change.
- **Scores:** `REVOKE SELECT ON users` and `GRANT SELECT (every other column)`
  to anon, authenticated and `prisma_app`. `prisma_app` had its own SELECT on
  the whole table (`app_roles`), so Prisma would still have read every score
  of the tenant. The list of columns is explicit: a column added to `users`
  later is unreadable by anon, authenticated and `prisma_app` until a
  migration grants it (a rule of `CLAUDE.md`). `talent_scores(ids)` answers own scores and those of the population
  of `ec.manage_employees` until Phase 18a gives them a key. The dashboard and
  the profile read their own through it, and every `select` on `users` names
  its columns.
- **`ec.view_compensation`:** the compensation section of the employee detail
  had no key of its own ("admin or team"). It now asks `can()` for
  `ec.view_compensation` on the employee. Owner and HR Administrator get it
  with `all` and Manager with `direct_reports`, in the seed, in the tenant
  sign-up (`create-tenant.ts`) and, for tenants that exist, in the migration.
- **Writes do not change.** They still go through `insert_effective_record`
  (by `app_role`) and the existing write policies: Known Risk below, Phase 11.

**Behavior that changes:**
- An administrator whose roles lack a data key sees only their own rows of it
  (an `is_admin_role` role with only `admin_center.access` reads no one
  else's job, pay, skills, goals, feedback, ARCO requests or audit log).
- HR Administrator and Owner hold no `pmgm.view_team_feedback` in the seed, so
  they no longer read other people's feedback. PRIVATE feedback is only for
  its author and its subject. Decision (least privilege): they stay without
  it. Once Admin Center edits RBP (Phase 11), each tenant decides whether to
  grant it; Phase 19 (CPM) defines who in HR reads non-private feedback.
- Managers now read the goals (`pmgm.manage_team_goals`) and the non-private
  feedback (`pmgm.view_team_feedback`) of their direct reports, which their
  role already granted and the policies did not allow.
- `syncLmsUsers()` and `getEmployeeCompletions()` read `lms_user_map` with the
  session's client. Nothing in the app calls them yet; when something does,
  the caller needs `admin.manage_roles` (or the worker, D17).
- With `all`, `can()` does not check that the target belongs to the tenant; RLS
  does, and the comparison test covers it with an employee of another tenant.

**Tests:**
- `__tests__/integration/rbp-reads.test.ts` compares, for every table above
  and every account, the exact set of rows visible through the Data API (the
  account's token) and through Prisma (`prisma_app`, the role of
  `DATABASE_URL`, with the same JWT claims and no `SET ROLE`) with the rows of
  the account's tenant that are its own or that the real `can()` allows. The
  accounts: an HR administrator (`all`), a manager (`direct_reports`), the
  report, a peer (`self`), a department viewer (`same_department`), an owner
  whose `is_admin_role` role has no data key, and an employee of another
  tenant. Feedback rows cover the three visibilities.
  - It runs twice, with the modules on and with `ec`, `pmgm` and `tih` off
    (only own rows remain for their keys).
  - `talent_scores()` and `directory_entries()` (42501 without
    `ec.view_org_chart`) are compared the same way. The three closed tables
    answer nothing to anyone while postgres sees their rows.
  - Not vacuous: the HR administrator reads the whole tenant's compensation,
    the manager their report's, the others their own; the department viewer
    reads the peer's job and not the report's; `performance_score` as a column
    gets 42501 by the Data API and by Prisma.
  - `rbp_module_active()` is checked against `PERMISSION_MODULE_MAP` one flag
    at a time.
  - Negative control, run by hand: with `ec_comp_access` set to `true` and
    `group_members_select` back to the tenant, exactly those two tables failed
    in both runs, along with the compensation check.
- The integration suites that call the real `can()` connect as `prisma_app`
  (`PRISMA_APP_TEST_DB_URL`) instead of the test role `prisma_test`, which
  cannot execute `rbp_context()`. `TestWorld` gives its admin the HR
  Administrator keys that guard reads and puts the manager in a Managers group
  with the seed's Manager keys over `direct_reports`, so the existing suites
  keep their intent; one of them counted the fixture's grants and members.
- `__tests__/static/no-select-star-users.test.ts`, with the TypeScript parser:
  it fails on `.from("users").select()` with no argument, with a top-level `*`
  or with an argument it cannot read, and on any `users(*)` embed, in every
  file of `src`. Negative and positive controls (`departments(*)` inside a
  users select, `*` of other tables, `superusers_log`).

**Performance:** the employee list, the dashboard and the profile, which k6
loads, now go through the new rule and functions. CI's short k6 run is the
measure (the local full profile is not comparable): p95 steady 108.5 ms on
`main` at 387e0d8 (D46) against the value in the D47 pull request; more than
20 % above, or above the 500 ms threshold, would block.

## D48 — Writing Rules: Neutral Address, Tú, Standard HR Labels

**Decision (9c PR 6a):** a text that greets or addresses a specific person,
or agrees with one, takes no gender: "Te damos la bienvenida, Ana", never
"Bienvenido, Ana" or "Estimada Ana". Role and field labels keep the standard
HR terms of Mexico that a SuccessFactors user recognizes: "Jefe directo",
"Empleados" (also the module's name), "administrador", "Evaluación del jefe
directo". Rewording them ("jefatura directa", "persona responsable") would
make the interface less clear. The interface speaks to the person as tú.

**What changed:**
- The home greeting: "Bienvenido, {name}" → "Te damos la bienvenida, {name}".
  No other greeting or form of address with a name existed; the emails
  already said "Hola {name}:" and "Te damos la bienvenida a {tenant}".
- Skills, the card of a direct report with no skills: "Este colaborador aún
  no ha registrado habilidades." → "Esta persona aún no ha registrado
  habilidades." It speaks of that person.
- The population `self`: "Uno mismo" → "Solo lo propio". The label was
  unclear, not a matter of gender.
- Tú: "No tiene permisos… Contacte al administrador" (the ARCO page), "Contacte
  al administrador" (no profile on the home page) and "Seleccione el tipo de
  acción" (the "Tomar Acción" dialog, which no longer asks for the type) were
  the only usted forms.
- Kept as they are, being labels of a role, a field or a value: "Jefe
  directo", "Solo jefe directo", "Evaluación del jefe directo", "Rechazada por
  administrador", "Rol de Administrador", "Número de empleado", and the
  readiness values "Listo ahora", "Listo en 1 año", "Listo en 2 años", "No
  listo".

**Tests (`__tests__/static/writing-rules.test.ts`):** over every string and
JSX text of `src`, read with the parser of D42 (now
`__tests__/static/visible-texts.ts`, shared by both tests).
- Address: "bienvenido/a/os/as" (not "la bienvenida", the noun) and, where a
  sentence starts, "estimado/a" and "querido/a" (so "Fecha estimada" is not a
  greeting). Role labels are not checked.
- Usted: the common imperatives ("seleccione", "ingrese", "contacte",
  "intente", "verifique", "escriba", "escanee", "haga clic", "comuníquese"…),
  "usted", "¿desea", "no tiene permisos" and "su contraseña/cuenta/sesión".
- Negative controls for each form and positive controls with the tú forms,
  the neutral greeting, the HR labels and impersonal texts ("La fecha de fin
  no puede ser anterior…"). Against the texts before this PR, both checks
  over `src` failed.

**Also in 9c PR 6a, one "Tomar Acción" button:** the employee detail showed
two. The menu loaded the lists and rendered `ActionModal`, which brought its
own "Tomar Acción" button and asked for the type of change again (one option
still said "Cambio de Puesto / Job Change"). Now:
- The menu's option opens the dialog straight on that form. The dialog is
  the `Dialog` of D43 (it was a hand-made `<dialog>` with no name and a "✕"
  without one): its title names the change and the employee ("Cambio de
  puesto — Patricia Castro"), and its close button has a name and 44 px.
- On close (Esc, the close button or after saving), the focus goes back to
  the "Tomar Acción" button. The option that opened it is gone by then, so
  the menu does it, not the browser.
- e2e (`dialogs.spec.ts`), with the keyboard at 1280 px light and 360 px
  dark: one "Tomar Acción" button; Enter, Tab and Enter open "Cambio de
  puesto — …" with the form and without the type question; it is modal; Esc
  closes it and the focus is back on the button; axe with it open in light
  and dark. The save on demo data (`save-demo-data.spec.ts`, D46) follows the
  new path.

## D49 — Keyboard Patterns: Menu Button, Combobox, ⌘K and Icon-Only Navigation at 200 %

**Decision (9c PR 6b):** the hand-made menus and lists of the shell follow
the ARIA patterns they look like, with the keyboard alone, and the icon-only
navigation works with the root font size at 200 %, which D44 had left open.
The `motion/react` license is in D51 (PR 6d); 6c became the privacy of searches: D27, D38 and this decision.

**"Tomar Acción", a menu button:** the dropdown was a list of buttons under a
button with no state.
- Now the button has `aria-haspopup="menu"`, `aria-expanded` and
  `aria-controls`, and the menu is `role="menu"` with `menuitem`s (named by
  the button).
- Enter, Space or Down open it on the first option and Up on the last; the
  arrows wrap; Home and End go to the ends.
- Esc closes it and gives the focus back to the button. Tab, a click outside
  or the focus leaving it close it (before, only a mousedown outside did).
- The button is the `Button` of D43 (44 px) and its options are 44 px. While
  `loadActionOptions()` answers, the button shows its loading state
  (`aria-busy`, disabled), so a second click starts nothing.

**PersonSearch, an ARIA 1.2 combobox:** the input owns a listbox
(`aria-controls`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-activedescendant`).
- Down and Up move through the people and Enter picks one; the focus then
  goes to "Cambiar <persona>", and "Cambiar" brings the focus back to the
  search. Esc closes the list first, so a second Esc closes the dialog
  around it.
- The number of people found is read by a polite live region once the typing
  has settled for 600 ms, not on every key.
- A 429 of `/api/colleagues` shows "Demasiados intentos. Intenta de nuevo en
  N segundos." from its Retry-After (the D28 text, now in
  `lib/rate-limit-message.ts` so a client component can use it), and any
  other failure "No se pudo buscar"; neither is shown as "no results".
- The input, the options and "Cambiar" are 44 px; so is the employee list's
  search box. Its texts moved to `lib/i18n/es-MX.ts`.

**⌘K:** Ctrl+K or ⌘K anywhere, or the header's "Buscar" button (44 px),
opens a `Dialog` (D43) with a combobox over two groups.
- **Ir a:** the menu's destinations, the same list already filtered by
  module and RBP.
- **Personas:** with 2 letters or more, people of the tenant from
  `/api/colleagues` (rate limited, the same messages as PersonSearch). Only
  for whoever can open the directory (`ec.view_org_chart`).
- A person opens the directory on that person, whose row opens the profile
  only when the person is in the reader's population (D47). Going straight to
  the profile would show "Acceso denegado" to whoever cannot open it; doing it
  right needs a permission check per result in `/api/colleagues`, left for
  when ⌘K gets actions (Phase 10 or 11).
- **Since 9c PR 6c, by id:** the URL is `/employees?person=<uuid>` (it was
  `?q=<correo>`).
  - The page reads the id with `z.guid()` and filters by it inside the
    caller's tenant (RLS too); the search box does not apply then.
  - An invalid value, an email or a repeated parameter is an empty id list:
    the page shows "No se encontró a esa persona", never the whole list. So
    does a person of another tenant.
  - "Filtrado por persona · Quitar filtro" goes back to the full list.
  - Tests: unit (the parameter), integration against the local database
    (a person of the tenant, of another tenant, an invalid value and an email,
    with and without a search), and e2e (⌘K opens `?person=` with no email in
    the URL; an invalid id or an email show no one).
- Actions ("Tomar Acción para…") come with the workflow engine (Phase 10) or
  the Admin Center (Phase 11).

**Icon-only navigation at 200 %:** the collapsed sidebar and the rail now
scroll their list in its own box, so every module and "Cerrar sesión" stay
reachable when the root font size doubles.
- The tooltips moved to the top layer with the Popover API, where no
  scrolling box cuts them (the reason D44 left this open). They are placed
  next to their control, inside the window, and follow it while it scrolls.
- They follow WCAG 1.4.13: they show on hover and on keyboard focus
  (`:focus-visible`), stay while the pointer moves onto them (a 150 ms grace
  period), and Esc hides them without moving the pointer or the focus;
  pointing at the control again shows them again.
- `role="tooltip"`, linked to the control with `aria-describedby`; the
  control's name is still its visually hidden text.

**Tests (e2e against the production image):**
- `dialogs.spec.ts`, the menu button with the keyboard:
  - the button's attributes and 44 px;
  - Enter, the arrows with wrapping, Home and End;
  - axe with the menu open;
  - Esc gives the focus back; Up opens it on the last option; Tab, Space and
    a click outside behave as described.
- `dialogs.spec.ts`, the loading state: the Server Action is held for a
  second; the button is `aria-busy` and disabled, and a forced second click
  starts no second load.
- `search.spec.ts`, PersonSearch in the feedback dialog:
  - 44 px, the listbox and `aria-activedescendant`;
  - the polite count, axe;
  - Esc closes the list and not the dialog;
  - Enter picks a person and the focus goes to "Cambiar".
- `search.spec.ts`, a 429 shows the safe message in the page and in the live
  region, and "No se encontraron personas" does not appear.
- `search.spec.ts`, ⌘K for the employee and the admin: Ctrl+K, a destination
  chosen with the keyboard, a person chosen with the keyboard opening the
  directory, axe with each group, and Esc giving the focus back to "Buscar".
- `navigation.spec.ts`, the collapsed sidebar at 1280 px and the rail at
  768 px with the root at 200 %:
  - Tab reaches every entry and each one is inside the window;
  - its tooltip opens on focus and stays inside the window;
  - Esc hides it with the focus kept;
  - hover shows it, the pointer can rest on it, Esc hides it under the
    pointer, and leaving hides it.

## D50 — Agent Tooling (ECC): Minimal Profile, No Hooks, CLAUDE.md First

**Decision:** ECC v2.2.3 (the `ecc-universal` package of
github.com/affaan-m/ECC) is installed per project in `.claude/` with
`npx ecc-universal@2.2.3 install --profile minimal --target claude-project`.
The minimal profile has no hook runtime and no hook is enabled: no settings
file registers one and `.claude/settings.json` does not exist. Of ECC's rules,
only security and TypeScript stay. `CLAUDE.md` prevails over every ECC rule,
agent, command and skill; `.claude/rules/README.md` says so, and Claude Code
loads it with the rules. Turning on any hook, from ECC or elsewhere, takes its
own pull request.

**What stays:** the rules `common/security.md`, `typescript/coding-style.md`,
`typescript/security.md`, `typescript/testing.md` and ECC's rules README; 28
agents, 44 commands and 41 skills. `.claude/ecc/install-state.json` lists the
182 files ECC manages, and `ecc doctor --target claude-project` reports OK.

**Removed because they contradict `CLAUDE.md`:**
1. `common/patterns.md` and `typescript/patterns.md`: the standard repository
   has a physical `delete(id)`. `CLAUDE.md` allows soft delete only, with no
   DELETE policy for `authenticated` (D46).
2. `common/testing.md`: TDD, 80 % coverage and unit, integration and E2E tests
   are mandatory for every change, and nothing says what `CLAUDE.md` requires:
   RLS, privileges and FKs tested against the real local database
   (`supabase start`), not only with mocks.
3. `common/code-review.md`: a merge "with caution" is allowed with HIGH
   findings. `CLAUDE.md` requires zero errors, fixed in the same loop.
4. `common/coding-style.md`, with `code-review.md`: immutability marked
   CRITICAL, functions under 50 lines and files under 800, modules to
   extract. They ask for refactors nobody requested; `CLAUDE.md` says to match
   the existing style and not to refactor what is not broken.
5. `common/coding-style.md`: errors handled "at every level". `CLAUDE.md`: no
   error handling for impossible cases.
6. `common/patterns.md`: a repository "to swap data sources", one envelope for
   every API response, cloned skeleton projects as a base. That is flexibility
   nobody asked for.
7. `common/development-workflow.md`: before coding, searches on GitHub, Exa
   and the package registries, and a PRD, architecture, system_design,
   tech_doc and task_list. `CLAUDE.md` works only against the local environment
   and asks for a brief plan with a check for each step.

**Also removed:**
- Rules that do not apply or are wrong here: `common/performance.md` (model
  choice and thinking), `common/agents.md` (an `ecc:` prefix these agents do
  not have, 68 agents, agents that are gone), `common/hooks.md` and
  `typescript/hooks.md` (hooks in the global `~/.claude/settings.json`, and
  Prettier, which the repo does not use), `common/git-workflow.md` (it says
  `includeCoAuthoredBy` is false and has no draft pull request or approval),
  and every language other than TypeScript.
- `.claude/settings.json`: the installer wrote `"includeCoAuthoredBy": false`.
  Commit attribution stays as the repo had it.
- The skill `codebase-onboarding`, whose template says `npx prisma migrate
  dev` (prohibited by D1); the skill `ecc-recipes` and `.claude/AGENTS.md`,
  which list agents and commands that are gone; the commands `orch-build-mvp`
  (it needs the GAN harness) and `plan-prd` (it hands off to `/plan`).
- The other five `orch-*` commands (`orch-add-feature`, `orch-change-feature`,
  `orch-fix-defect`, `orch-refine-code`, `orch-review`): they start `orch-*`
  skills that the minimal profile does not install, so they did nothing. The
  skill `skill-scout`: it searches GitHub with `gh search repos` and
  `gh search code`, remote traffic (Roadmap, Principle 8).
- The skills `codehealth-mcp` (CodeScene's MCP server, with its own token) and
  `repo-scan` (it clones a skill from GitHub), and the agent `docs-lookup`
  (Context7's MCP server): remote traffic (Roadmap, Principle 8).
- Agents and commands of other stacks (C++, C#, Dart, Django, FastAPI,
  Flutter, F#, Go, HarmonyOS, Java, Kotlin and Gradle, PHP, Python, PyTorch,
  Rust, Swift, Vue) and domains (healthcare, homelab, networks, marketing, SEO,
  ML, RAG, open-source publishing, the GAN harness); the commands `jira`,
  `pm2`, `marketing-campaign`, `epic-*`, `santa-loop`, `auto-update` (with its
  script), `cost-report`, `model-route` and `setup-pm`; the skill
  `windows-desktop-e2e`.
- The commands `code-review` and `plan`, which collide with Claude Code's own.
- `.claude/mcp-configs/`, a catalog of 34 remote MCP servers. None was enabled:
  project MCP servers would go in `.mcp.json`, which does not exist.
- `.claude/.agents/`, skills in Codex's format that Claude Code does not load;
  one had a sample API key that gitleaks reports.

Each removal also left `install-state.json`, so `ecc doctor` finds no missing
or drifted file and `ecc repair` does not bring them back. The descriptions of
`skill-create` and `review-pr` lost their references to removed items
(`skill-scout`, `/orch-review`, `/code-review`), and `browser-qa` lost its
line for `mcp__browserbase__*` (a paid remote browser, Roadmap, Principle 8)
and its mention of `/canary-watch`, a command that does not exist here. Their
digests in `install-state.json` were updated to the edited files. Running the install
command again would bring everything back; an ECC update goes through a pull
request that repeats these removals and edits.

**Known limits:**
- `install-state.json` records absolute paths of the container where the
  install ran (`/home/user/ScaliaHR/.claude/…`). From a checkout at another
  path, `ecc doctor` reports ERROR (182 unsafe destinations) although the files
  match.
- `typescript/coding-style.md` and `typescript/testing.md` start with a link to
  `common/coding-style.md` and `common/testing.md`, now removed. Claude Code
  does not follow Markdown links in rules, so nothing breaks; the links lead
  nowhere. `typescript/coding-style.md` still has an immutability example;
  `CLAUDE.md` prevails over it.
- The installer also wrote ECC's state store, `~/.claude/ecc/state.db`, in the
  home directory of the machine where it ran. It is not part of the repo.

**Checks:** eslint and tsc in `apps/web`, `packages/email` and `apps/worker`,
the build, `check:forbidden` and gitleaks over the whole history, all at zero.
Outside `.claude/`, only this document and `docs/ROADMAP.md` change.

## D51 — motion/react With Reduced Motion, the Dashboard Grid and Long Forms in Steps

**Decision (9c PR 6d):** animations in React go through `motion/react`, only
in client components and only through `m.*` from `motion/react-m`, under one
`MotionProvider` in the protected layout. Its animation features load with
`import()` after the page, so they stay out of the initial JS. The same pull
request gives the dashboard its grid and splits "Actualizar información
personal" into steps. D49 and D52 had kept this number for it.

**Package and license:** `motion` 14.14.0, a dependency of `apps/web`, brings
`framer-motion` 14.14.0, `motion-dom` 14.14.0 and `motion-utils` 14.7.0. All
four are MIT, ship in the browser bundle and are not modified. The lockfile
adds only those four packages.

**Initial JS:** `check:prod-build` now measures, for each page, the gzip size
(level 9) of the JS its first load needs: the build manifest's
`rootMainFiles` plus the chunks of the page and of every layout, template,
loading, error and not-found segment above it, each file once. Next's "First
Load JS" table leaves out the layout chunks, which is where the provider
lives.
- It prints the largest page, and `--json <file>` writes every page's size.
- With `--baseline <json>` (an earlier `--json`) it also prints the largest
  growth and a `::warning::` for each page that grows more than 30 kB, the
  budget the ROADMAP set.
- The initial JS never fails the build. An unreadable baseline is reported
  as "not measured"; a flag without its file, or an unknown flag, is a usage
  error (exit 2).
- CI runs it without a baseline: comparing with `main` needs a build of
  `main`, so a pull request that adds client code measures it by hand, as this
  one did.

Measured on this pull request's final tree against `main` at 3a624f6:
- Protected pages: +18.6 to +18.8 kB (191.9 → 210.5 kB on `/dashboard`).
  `/employees/[id]`, which also carries the steps, grows 20.3 kB (197.2 →
  217.5 kB).
- `/login`, `/` and not-found: +0.1 to +0.3 kB, with no provider.
- The features chunk (`domAnimation`, with the animation and exit code) is
  18.8 kB gzip and loads after the page.

**Reduced motion, and features that have not loaded:**
- `MotionConfig reducedMotion="user"` turns transforms off with
  `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, and the provider sets every transition to
  0 s when `useReducedMotion()` is true, as the D40 tokens do in CSS.
- The provider also gives `useMotionOn()`: true once the features have loaded
  and reduce is off. Components use a start or exit state only when it is true
  (otherwise `initial={false}` and no `exit`), so the element is painted in its
  final state in its first frame. With reduce nothing moves or fades.
- Before the features load, or if their chunk fails, an `m.*` element cannot
  animate. Without this flag a start state such as `opacity: 0` would stay on
  screen, and the toast or the next step would be invisible.
- Durations and easing are the D40 tokens (`MOTION_SECONDS`, `EASE_STANDARD`).
  A unit test keeps them equal to `globals.css`.

**Where it is used:**
- The toast (D43) fades in and slides 8 px down, then fades out, inside
  `AnimatePresence`. Its `m.div` only animates; the pause on hover and focus
  and Esc stay on the plain `div` inside it.
- Each new step of the personal information form fades in and slides 8 px up.
- The native `<dialog>` (D43) opens and closes without animation, so its focus
  trap, Esc and focus return stay the browser's.

**CSP:** unchanged. motion writes styles through the CSSOM, and the start state
that server rendering puts in a `style` attribute is already allowed by
`style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'` (D29).

**Long forms in steps:** "Actualizar información personal" has 14 fields and
now has three steps, Nombre, Contacto, and Domicilio y motivo
(`PERSONAL_INFO_STEPS`).
- Each step has an `h3`, "Paso N de 3: …", with `tabIndex={-1}`. It takes the
  focus when the step changes, forward or with "Anterior", not when the dialog
  opens.
- Every step stays mounted (the others `hidden`), so what was typed is kept
  and the last submit reads the whole form.
- Going forward checks only the visible step, with the browser's validity
  (required field left empty, malformed email). Errors show next to each field
  (`aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`), a count ("Revisa 1 campo de este
  paso.") is announced in a `role="alert"` that is always in the page, and the
  focus goes to the first invalid field.
- The last step sends the whole object once. The Server Action keeps its order
  (rate limit, zod over the whole object, actor, permission, RPC). When zod
  fails on form fields it returns the errors by field (`fieldErrors`, only the
  schema's messages, never internal ones, D27) with "Revisa los campos
  marcados.", and the
  form goes back to the first step with an error and focuses its title. An
  error that no field shows (the employee's id) keeps the earlier single
  message, and it is left out of `fieldErrors` when fields fail too.
- Any other failure shows in a second `role="alert"`: the action's message,
  or "No se pudieron guardar los cambios. Intenta de nuevo." when the action
  throws (network failure, server crash). A request that never answers has no
  timeout: the button stays loading. The saved message uses a
  `role="status"`. All three regions are always in the page and only their
  text changes, so screen readers announce it.
- After a save nothing more is sent during the saved message's 1.2 s, and a
  dialog closed in that time does not close a later one.
- "Anterior" clears the error count, which only describes the step it was
  shown on.
- One primary button whose label changes ("Siguiente", then "Guardar cambios"),
  so the one-primary rule of D43 holds.
- The job change and compensation forms are short and keep one step.

**Dashboard grid:** "Mi Información" and "Mis Objetivos (PMGM)" sit in a grid of
`repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, 26rem), 1fr))`: side by side at 1280 px and
in one column at 768 and 360 px. Columns are in `rem`, as D44 asks of grids.
With two widgets the grid has one or two columns; more columns come with more
widgets.

**Known limits:**
- No layout animations, `popLayout` or shared-element transitions: they need
  more features. No motion in the login, which has no provider, or in server
  components (the static test requires `"use client"`).
- Every protected page downloads the features chunk after hydration, even
  when nothing animates on it.
- If the features chunk fails to load, nothing animates and LazyMotion's
  promise, which has no catch, is reported in the browser console. The page
  works without motion.
- The dialog opens without the employee's current personal data
  (`action-menu.tsx` passes `currentPersonal={null}`, as before this PR).
- In the image build, `turbo prune` leaves out `apps/mobile` and its
  `@types/react` 18, the copy that motion's types resolve at the root. There
  motion's props are untyped, so a handler on an `m.*` element gets an
  implicit `any` and the build fails; handlers go on plain elements. The local
  and CI typecheck, with the full install, type them.
- The buttons' loading indicator (D43) is CSS (`motion-safe:animate-spin`), not
  motion/react.

**Tests:**
- Static: `motion-imports.test.ts` (`"use client"`, no `framer-motion` and no
  `motion.*`, one provider, the tokens equal to `globals.css`), with a negative
  control.
- Unit: every field in exactly one step, `firstStepWithError`, `toFieldErrors`;
  the action returns the errors by field without reaching the actor or the
  RPC, keeps the single message for the employee's id, and saves a valid
  object with one RPC; the initial JS of `check:prod-build` (layouts counted,
  each file once, CSS and polyfills left out, growth and warning, CLI).
- Static: the features load only through `import()` in `motion.tsx`, nothing
  else imports them or `domAnimation`, and the protected layout wraps the
  toasts in `MotionProvider`.
- DOM (jsdom): the focus on a step change only, the alert and `aria-invalid`,
  a server error back to its step, a failure no field explains and an action
  that throws, nothing sent after a save, no `onDone` after unmount, the count
  cleared by "Anterior", and the saved message before closing.
- e2e on the production image: the steps with the keyboard at 360, 768 and
  1280 px, with axe in light and dark on each step and on the server's error,
  and captures (`captures/pasos/`); with and without
  `prefers-reduced-motion`, frame sampling of the new step and of the toast
  (with reduce no frame moves or fades) and the spinner of "Tomar Acción"
  (`spin` without reduce, `none` with it); the dashboard grid at the three
  widths.

**Checks:** eslint and tsc in `apps/web`, `packages/email` and `apps/worker`
at zero (`@scaliahr/mobile` keeps its one earlier warning), and tsc of
`apps/web` also without the root `@types/react`, as in the image. Unit tests:
web 1593, worker 46, email 34. `next build`, `check:prod-build`,
`check:forbidden` and `check-turbo-version` at zero. The full e2e (73 tests,
161 axe scans, no violation) against the production image on Supabase local,
and no axe or Playwright in the image. The captures of the steps and of the
dashboard at 360, 768 and 1280 px are in `docs/capturas/9c-pr6d/`.

## D52 — ECC Agents in the Loops: One Stage Each, Reviewers Only Report

**Decision:** `CLAUDE.md` has a section, "Agentes ECC en los loops
(.claude/agents)", that gives each of the 28 agents D50 kept a stage in the
loop, or says it runs only when the user asks, or not at all. It also lists the
ECC commands and skills that are not used. That section is the rule; this entry
records why and its limits. D51 is `motion/react` (9c PR 6d).

**Precedence:** `CLAUDE.md` prevails over every ECC agent, skill, command and
rule (D50). A suggestion that contradicts it is dropped and written in the
pull request's verification table. Review agents only report: the main loop
makes every fix, surgically and inside the pull request's scope. A finding
outside that scope is written down as an observation with its phase, not
fixed.

**Stages:**
1. Understand and plan, read-only, before any code: `code-explorer`,
   `planner` (a brief plan with checks, no PRD), and `architect` and
   `code-architect` only for a new D decision or a new pattern.
2. Implement: `build-error-resolver` and `react-build-resolver`, minimal diffs
   only.
3. Review, in parallel over `git diff` against main, before the pull request
   is opened or updated:
   - Always: `code-reviewer`, `typescript-reviewer`, `security-reviewer`,
     `silent-failure-hunter` and `pr-test-analyzer`.
   - Depending on the diff: `database-reviewer`, `react-reviewer`,
     `a11y-architect`, `type-design-analyzer`, `comment-analyzer` and
     `performance-optimizer`.
   - A critical or high finding is fixed, and the agent that found it runs
     again until it reports none.
   - The verification table has one row per agent.
4. E2E: `e2e-runner`, only when UI flows change, with local Playwright. Tests
   really run, and none is quarantined or disabled to get CI green.

**Only when the user asks:** `tdd-guide`, `code-simplifier` and
`refactor-cleaner` (only in a cleanup pull request of their own) and
`doc-updater`.

**Not used:** the agents `chief-of-staff`, `conversation-analyzer`,
`harness-optimizer`, `loop-operator`, `spec-miner` and `agent-evaluator`, 21
commands and 5 skills, listed in `CLAUDE.md`. They stay installed, so
`ecc doctor` still matches `install-state.json`.

**Why:**
- Some agents' defaults contradict `CLAUDE.md`, and the section names the
  rule that wins for each:
  - `e2e-runner` prefers Vercel Agent Browser (installed with
    `npm install -g agent-browser`) and quarantines flaky tests.
  - `tdd-guide` requires 80 % coverage.
  - `doc-updater` generates `docs/CODEMAPS/`.
- If reviewers only report, one author owns the diff: every fix goes through
  the same checks and stays in scope.
- By their own descriptions, what is not used works outside a pull request's
  scope:
  - `chief-of-staff` triages email and chat.
  - `conversation-analyzer` feeds `/hookify`, and there are no hooks (D50).
  - `harness-optimizer` tunes the agent harness, and `loop-operator`,
    `/loop-start` and `/loop-status` drive autonomous loops.
  - `spec-miner` writes `openspec/specs/`.
  - `agent-evaluator` grades agent output.
  - `/pr` and `/prp-pr` push and run `gh pr create` on their own, outside the
    draft-and-approval flow.
  - `/checkpoint` makes a git stash or commit and writes
    `.claude/checkpoints.log`.
  - `/quality-gate` is a Biome or Prettier check meant to run as a hook, and
    the repo uses neither.
  - `/security-scan` runs `npx ecc-agentshield`, a package outside the
    lockfile.
  - `/hookify*` writes hook rules.
  - `/learn*`, `/evolve`, `/instinct-*`, `/promote`, `/prune` and the
    `continuous-learning` skills write learned instincts and skills; v2
    observes sessions through hooks.
  - `/update-docs` and `/update-codemaps` rewrite documentation.
  - `unified-memory` keeps a vault in `~/.ecc/memory/`, outside the repo.
  - `council` and `santa-method` add more multi-agent passes on top of
    stage 3.

**Known limits:**
- This is an instruction, not a control: nothing checks that a loop ran the
  agents its diff called for. The person who approves reads the verification
  table.
- `a11y-architect` and `performance-optimizer` only report, yet both keep
  `Write` and `Edit` in their `tools:`. That holds only as long as the loop's
  prompt tells them so. Removing those tools changes files that
  `install-state.json` tracks, so it takes its own pull request, as D50's
  edits did.
- 15 of the 28 agent descriptions say "Use PROACTIVELY" or "MUST BE USED".
  That includes `tdd-guide`, `refactor-cleaner` and `doc-updater`, which run
  only when the user asks. Claude Code may delegate to an agent on its own
  because of its description; `CLAUDE.md` prevails.
- The other 23 commands and 36 skills are not named and stay as D50 left them.
  One of them, `/prp-prd`, writes a PRD, which `planner` must not.

**Checks:** eslint and tsc in `apps/web`, `packages/email` and `apps/worker`,
the build, `check:forbidden` and gitleaks over the whole history, all at zero.
Only `CLAUDE.md`, this document and `docs/ROADMAP.md` change.

---

## D53 — One List of Log Masks for the Web App and the Worker

**Decision:** The patterns that mask free text in the logs live in one module,
`packages/email/src/log-masks.ts`, exported as `@scaliahr/email/log-masks`
(`maskText`). The web app's `redactString()` (D27) and the worker's `mask()`
both call it. Until this decision the worker kept its own copy of the list,
without email addresses or Bearer/Basic tokens, while its comment and D27 said
it used the same masks. `errorFields()` logs `error.message`, and an SMTP
rejection can quote the recipient.

**How each app consumes it:**
- The web app reads it from source, through a tsconfig path and a vitest
  alias, like `@scaliahr/email/contract`. The module has no imports, so only
  that file enters the server and Edge bundles. None of it reaches the
  browser: a one-time `grep -rlF` of `.next/static` for `[EMAIL]`, `[CLABE]`,
  `maskText` and `log-masks` found nothing, on main and on this change. No CI
  step checks it.
- The worker reads it from `dist`, through the package's `exports` entry
  `./log-masks`. The worker's Dockerfile already builds `@scaliahr/email` and
  copies its `dist`, so the image gains one small file and no dependency.
- The Dockerfiles, `turbo prune`, CI, `next.config.ts` and the lockfile do not
  change.

**The list:** the web app's eight patterns, byte for byte and in its order.
Email addresses go first, because otherwise the NSS and RFC masks split an
address and leave its domain (`[NSS]@acme.dev`). Then JWT, Bearer/Basic, CURP,
RFC, CLABE, card numbers and NSS. The web app's output does not change; the
worker gains the email and token masks.

**Options weighed:**
- A subpath of `@scaliahr/email` (chosen): no infrastructure change, because
  it is the one package both apps already consume.
- A subpath of `@scaliahr/shared`: the worker does not depend on it, its
  `dist` is not built for Node's ESM, and it would bring
  `@supabase/supabase-js` into the worker image.
- A package of its own: the best cohesion, at the cost of a new workspace, its
  builds in CI and in the worker's Dockerfile, and new lockfile entries.
- Two lists with a parity test: no infrastructure either, but every change is
  made twice.

**Limits:**
- Cohesion: log masks sit in the email package. A package of their own is the
  way out if the module grows.
- The module must stay free of imports, because the web app bundles it for
  the Edge runtime.
- `\w` is ASCII only: an address with a non-ASCII local part or an IDN domain
  is not masked, in either app.
- Only the patterns are shared. The web app also blanks sensitive keys and
  masks `stack` and `cause`; the worker logs only an error's name, message
  and code.
- An address whose domain has no dot, such as `user@localhost`, is not
  masked: the pattern needs a dot after the `@`. Accepted by the user's
  decision in #78.

**Checks:** worker unit tests that failed before the change and pass after:
an SMTP rejection quoting `ana.lopez@acme.dev`, `Authorization: Bearer
abc.def.ghi`, and an address the NSS mask used to split. The web app's
redaction tests are unchanged and green. The patterns are byte-identical to
the web app's on main, the one-time check of `.next/static` found no mask
string, and lint, tsc
and build are at zero in the web app, the worker and the email package.

---

## D54 — Diagnostics of the First k6 Attempt in CI

**Decision:** the `docker` job prints one "D36-diag:" line per diagnostic, in
the log and in the job summary, uploads an artifact per attempt and runs a
second, informational k6 pass. The goal is to explain why a pull request's
first attempt tends to be slower than its rerun (#76 111.3 → 63.11 ms, #77
120.28 → 69.37 ms, #78 123.28 → 51.95 ms, from the verification tables of
those pull requests), and why `main`, which only has first attempts, gave
91.5, 108.81 and 72.57 ms (28ab653, 839eb08 and 6a33b6a). This is part A of
the D36 investigation: instrumentation only. Part B (warm-up, window, `HOLD`,
baseline) is a later decision. Phase 9c, before its PR 6d (`motion/react`,
ROADMAP).
- Part B is D56 (2026-11-20): the gate is now the A/B ratio of four k6 passes
  on the same runner, `main`, PR, PR, `main`, and this second pass is the PR's
  second one (`pr-2`).

**What does not change:** the window, `RAMP`, `HOLD`, the phase rule, the
thresholds, the noise band and its limits, and the number D36 reads: the
`{ kind:page,phase:steady }` line of the first pass, in the step "k6 load
test, short run (D36)". That step still fails the job on its own thresholds,
as before.
- Two things are added to the first pass, and neither is a page request:
  - k6 writes every sample to a CSV (`--out csv=…`, `timeFormat=unix_milli`).
    The writer runs in the background while k6 measures. Its cost cannot be
    shown to be zero, but from now on it is the same in every run, `main`'s
    included.
  - The sign-in request carries its phase, a `sign_in_retries` counter counts
    the retries, and four listing thresholds (`count>=0`) put both in the
    summary. The submetric D36 reads selects `kind:page` samples only, so it
    holds the same samples as before.

**The diagnostics never fail the job:** their steps, the artifact's
included, have `continue-on-error`. A diagnostic that cannot produce its
line prints an `::error::` annotation instead of a guessed value, and the
step shows as failed. The diagnostic steps after the D36 run also run when
that run failed, as long as it left its summary: the slow attempts are the
ones to explain. (The job's other later steps, such as the per-tenant export
and the rollback drill, still skip after a failure.)
- One exception: a page of the second pass that showed another tenant's
  employees fails the job, in its own step ("Tenant isolation held in the
  second k6 pass (D54)"), because that is a tenant-isolation failure, not a
  diagnostic.
  - The count comes from the second pass's summary as soon as it can be
    read, whatever k6's exit code.
  - If the second pass leaves no readable count (it crashed before writing
    its summary), that step only warns. The first pass does not fully cover
    isolation then: its checks threshold (> 99 %) tolerates up to 1 % failed
    checks, isolation ones included.
    - Since #81 the first pass has no tolerance either: its isolation check
      has a threshold of its own (`rate==1`, D36), so a single page with
      another tenant's employees fails the first pass, and the job, within
      what the check sees (D36).
    - Since D56 the ratio step fails the job when any of the four passes,
      this one included, has no isolation count (D56, condition 3).
- Since D56 the first pass and this second pass are also `pr-1` and `pr-2`
  of D56's ratio. The step "The PR's k6 passes for D56 (pr-1, pr-2)" checks
  them as D56 checks every pass (k6's exit code, the steady samples, the CSV
  against the summary, the run's thresholds) and fails the job when one is
  broken, as "pasada del PR inválida". The diagnostic steps themselves still
  never fail it.
  Since `warm-pr-1` (2026-11-22) that step also checks `pr-1`'s warm-up, so it
  can fail as "calentamiento del PR roto" or "pasadas no comparables" too.

**The lines** (`scripts/d36-diag.mjs`), each one for one possible cause.
`diagLine` refuses any line off the grammar (a closed list of topics,
lower-case keys, only strings and finite numbers as values):
1. `runner`: CPU model (`lscpu`), `nproc`, `MemTotal` and
   `GITHUB_RUN_ATTEMPT`. Tests the hardware. A field it cannot read says
   "unavailable", with an `::warning::` that gives the reason.
2. `healthy to k6`: seconds from the web container turning healthy to k6's
   first request in the CSV. Tests the time since the stack came up.
   Since D56's warm-up of the PR's stack (2026-11-22), that time also holds
   `warm-pr-1`, the unmeasured k6 pass between the e2e and the D36 step.
   - The probe log is read in the step "First healthy probe of the web
     container (D54)", right after `local:up`. Docker keeps only the last
     five probes in `State.Health.Log` (50 s at the image's 10 s interval),
     and the e2e runs between that step and k6.
   - The time is the first passing probe after the last failing one.
     `exact=no` when the log holds five probes and none failed: older probes
     rolled out, so the change may be older.
3. `sign-ins`: per pass, the sign-ins and the retries in each phase. Tests
   whether sign-ins (bcrypt) fall inside the measured window. k6 exports the
   four submetrics even at 0, because of their thresholds, so a missing one
   is an error, not a 0.
4. `standalone`: two sha256 of the web image's `.next/standalone`. Tests
   whether two images hold the same code.
   - `scripts/d36-standalone-files.mjs` runs in the image of the measured
     container, offline, read-only and without capabilities. It lists every
     file of `/app` but `apps/web/.next/static` (copied in by the
     Dockerfile, not standalone) with its sha256, with BUILD_ID replaced by
     `<BUILD_ID>` in paths and contents, sorted by the replaced path's
     bytes. Symlinks are hashed by their target.
   - `sha256` covers every file. `sha256_without_manifests` leaves out the
     Next.js manifests: files under a `.next/` folder whose name ends in
     `manifest.json` or `manifest.js`.
5. `requests`: per pass, the CSV's rows and its steady-state page p95. The
   CSV can recompute the p95 with other windows without new runs.
   - Its p95, average and maximum must equal the same pass's summary within
     0.001 ms (`matches_summary=yes`). k6's percentile is linear
     interpolation between the closest ranks.
   - A row with another number of cells than the header, or a time or value
     that is not a number, is an error.
6. `second pass`: the same k6 run again, right after the first, on the same
   runner and stack, with the same settings, re-seeded like the first. Tests
   the cold-start hypothesis.
   - Since D56's `warm-pr-1` (2026-11-22) the first pass also follows a k6
     pass on the same stack, so the two passes compare a warm stack with a
     warm one, no longer a cold one with a warm one.
   - It prints its p95, failed requests, failed checks, the pages that
     showed another tenant's employees, k6's exit code, its seconds and
     `counts_for_d36=no`. Its k6 summary goes to `second-pass.txt` in the
     artifact, not to the log.
   - A crossed threshold (exit 99) is reported. Any other exit, or any page
     with another tenant's employees, fails the step; the isolation count,
     when the summary holds it, then fails the job in the step that follows
     the artifact. A failed run prints the tail of its output, indented and
     without any p95 line.

**Why two hashes (measured locally by this pull request's author, four builds
of the web app; the measurement is not stored in the repo):**
- Two `next build`s of the same commit, with BUILD_ID replaced, differed in
  24 of 2,091 files. All were manifests of the same size whose JSON keys came
  out in another order: `app-path-routes-manifest.json`,
  `server-reference-manifest.json` and the `*_client-reference-manifest.js`
  of each route. Without the manifests, no file differed. With the final
  scripts, two builds of the same tree gave different `sha256` and the same
  `sha256_without_manifests`.
- A build of 839eb08 against one of 6a33b6a (D53 in between) also differed
  without the manifests, where the code changed: the files that bundle
  `redact.ts` (the middleware, both instrumentation files, three routes and
  three chunks, one of them renamed), the trace files (`*.nft.json`) that
  list the new module, and `packages/email/package.json`, which the trace
  now brings into the standalone output.
- So `sha256` identifies one build, and `sha256_without_manifests` says
  whether two builds hold the same code, when both builds reuse Next.js's
  cache.
- In CI each attempt builds without that cache, and there it does not hold.
  The two attempts of #79 (same commit, dd61252) differed in 34 of 2,075
  files: 26 manifests and the `page.js` of the 8 routes with Server Actions
  (admin-center, ARCO, employee, both MFA pages, performance, skills hub and
  login). Two local builds after removing `.next` differed in the same 8.
  Those pages embed the Server Actions' ids and module ids, which Next.js
  generates again in a build without its cache.
  - In CI, then, the code is compared with the diff of the two
    `standalone-files.txt`: a diff of only manifests and those pages means
    the same code. Neither hash says it alone.

**Artifact:** `d36-diag-attempt-<n>`, kept 14 days: `summary.json`,
`summary-second.json`, `requests-first.csv.gz`, `requests-second.csv.gz`,
`second-pass.txt` and `standalone-files.txt`.
- An explicit list, never `load/.out` whole: `accounts.json` there holds the
  load accounts' emails and their shared password.
- `load/.out` is a hidden folder, which `upload-artifact` skips by default,
  so the step sets `include-hidden-files`, and it fails (without failing the
  job) if it finds no file.
- The CSV holds metric rows, URLs and tags; no bodies, headers or cookies.

**Cost:** estimated at about 100–115 s more per run of the `docker` job:
the second pass takes about as long as the D36 step, and the hash, the other
lines and the upload add about 10–20 s. The D36 step took 92 s on 6a33b6a
(run 44591133282: about 3 s of seed, 3 s to start k6 and 86 s of k6, from
the log's timestamps). The second pass is under 120 s, so it runs on every
pull request and on `main`, with no variable to switch it on. This pull
request's CI measures the real cost.

**Limits:**
- Diagnostics only: they show what differs between attempts, not what to
  change. Part B decides.
- A sign-in that answers 200 with a body that is not JSON ends the iteration
  without `sleep(5)`, and is not counted as a retry. Supabase Auth answers a
  token request with JSON, so this is not expected.
- The second pass re-seeds the load tenants: it repeats the first pass's
  procedure on a stack that has just served it, so it separates a warm stack
  from a cold one, not fresh data from old.
  Since D56's `warm-pr-1` (2026-11-22) the first pass also runs on a stack
  that a k6 pass has just served, so the second pass no longer separates a
  warm stack from a cold one either.
- The CSV reader expects k6 v1.8.2's columns; a k6 upgrade that changes them
  makes the `requests` and `healthy to k6` lines fail loudly, not misread.
  Since D56 such an upgrade also fails the job: D56 reads every pass's CSV
  with the same reader.

**Checks:**
- Unit tests (`apps/web/__tests__/scripts/d36-diag.test.ts`): the line
  grammar, the parsing behind each line, the hash on fixture trees (BUILD_ID
  in paths and contents, ids that sort before and after their siblings,
  symlinks), and `load/pages.js` run in Node, in strict mode, with stubbed k6
  modules (page parameters and tags, the phase boundary at `RAMP`, the
  sign-in tag, a retry).
- `scripts/load-test.sh` run with fake `supabase`, `curl`, `node` and
  `docker`: the summary goes to `/out/summary.json` by default, to the
  `LOAD_SUMMARY` file otherwise, and extra arguments reach k6 before the
  script.
- The new steps run as `ci.yml` writes them, with fake `docker` and `npm`:
  the second pass's steady line never reaches the log or the job summary,
  exit 0 and 99 pass and any other exit fails, and one failing diagnostic
  does not stop the others.
- Locally, not stored in the repo: k6 v1.8.2 (checked against the release's
  checksum) against a stub server, with `pages.js` before and after (the
  steady line is unchanged, the CSV reproduces the summary's percentiles, a
  submetric with no sample is exported at 0, and `root_group.checks` holds
  each check), and the builds above.
- CI: the lines of this pull request's attempts and of `main`.

**Provisional conclusion (#79, 2026-11-19):** inconclusive, but it points to
the runner's hardware (AMD EPYC 7763 against Intel Xeon 6975P-C) and not to a
cold start; more runs with D36-diag are needed to confirm it.
- On #79's four attempts the second pass, on the same runner, gave about the
  same p95 as the first: 55.41 and 53.51 ms on Intel, 93.29 and 119.52 ms on
  AMD.
- The p95 did not follow the seconds from healthy to k6 (392.8, 483.1, 273.9
  and 277.2 s): the two runs with the highest first-pass p95 (AMD, 98.13 and
  112.8 ms) had the fewest seconds (273.9 and 277.2 s).
- Correction (#81, 2026-11-20) to the bullet above: its argument does not
  hold.
  - On the four attempts it quotes (dd61252 and 0342b31, before #79's
    closing commit cb50dd3), that the two with the highest p95 had the
    fewest seconds is what a cold start would predict, not evidence against
    it. (Across the 12 attempts below it no longer holds: the first attempt
    of f70d96e, in #80, had 130.79 ms at 283.8 s.)
  - The seconds cannot separate a cold start from the hardware: on the 12
    attempts with D36-diag so far (#79, #80 and `main`, table below) they
    go with the runner's CPU model. 270.4–285.8 s on every attempt on AMD
    EPYC 7763 and Intel Xeon Platinum 8573C, 373.9–692.2 s on Intel Xeon
    6975P-C, and 220.7 s on the one attempt on AMD EPYC 9V45.
  - The p95 goes with the CPU model. Fast: Intel Xeon 6975P-C (46.34–61.61
    ms, six attempts) and AMD EPYC 9V45 (53.38 ms, one attempt, the one
    with the fewest seconds of all). Slow: AMD EPYC 7763 (98.13–130.79 ms,
    four attempts) and Intel Xeon Platinum 8573C (94.92 ms, one attempt).
    Within Intel Xeon 6975P-C (six attempts) the p95 shows no clear trend
    with the seconds: 46.34 ms at the fewest (373.9 s), 57.14 ms at the
    most (692.2 s).
  - The second pass, warm, on the same runner, never moved an attempt out
    of its CPU group: 43.96–59.00 ms on the fast models and 93.29–133.77 ms
    on the slow ones, against 46.34–61.61 and 94.92–130.79 ms in the first
    pass. It was faster than the first pass in 5 of the 11 attempts with
    both passes and slower in 6. That is consistent with no systematic
    warm-up gain in these attempts and with the hardware as the cause; it
    does not prove it.
  - The cause is to be confirmed in part B.
  - The first-pass p95 is the `{ kind:page,phase:steady }` line D36 reads;
    the CPU, the seconds and the second-pass p95 come from the attempt's
    "D36-diag:" lines. The first attempt of f70d96e was cancelled during its
    second pass.

  | Run (CI) | Head | Attempt | Runner CPU | Healthy to k6 | First pass p95 | Second pass p95 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 44602211249 | #79 dd61252 | 1 | Intel(R) Xeon(R) 6975P-C | 392.8 s | 54.73 ms | 55.41 ms |
  | 44602211249 | #79 dd61252 | 2 | Intel(R) Xeon(R) 6975P-C | 483.1 s | 61.61 ms | 53.51 ms |
  | 44608857876 | #79 0342b31 | 1 | AMD EPYC 7763 64-Core Processor | 273.9 s | 98.13 ms | 93.29 ms |
  | 44608857876 | #79 0342b31 | 2 | AMD EPYC 7763 64-Core Processor | 277.2 s | 112.8 ms | 119.52 ms |
  | 44617883823 | #79 cb50dd3 | 1 | INTEL(R) XEON(R) PLATINUM 8573C | 270.4 s | 94.92 ms | 94.53 ms |
  | 44620919184 | `main` 4dd2a75 | 1 | Intel(R) Xeon(R) 6975P-C | 373.9 s | 46.34 ms | 53.57 ms |
  | 44638464188 | #80 f70d96e | 1 | AMD EPYC 7763 64-Core Processor | 283.8 s | 130.79 ms | — (cancelled) |
  | 44638464188 | #80 f70d96e | 2 | Intel(R) Xeon(R) 6975P-C | 692.2 s | 57.14 ms | 58.51 ms |
  | 44638464188 | #80 f70d96e | 3 | Intel(R) Xeon(R) 6975P-C | 670.9 s | 51.45 ms | 43.96 ms |
  | 44640025060 | #80 43ba305 | 1 | AMD EPYC 7763 64-Core Processor | 285.8 s | 111.27 ms | 133.77 ms |
  | 44649927034 | #80 a647b85 | 1 | AMD EPYC 9V45 96-Core Processor | 220.7 s | 53.38 ms | 59.00 ms |
  | 44651711154 | `main` 13005c9 | 1 | Intel(R) Xeon(R) 6975P-C | 676.4 s | 54.52 ms | 52.49 ms |
